Uncertainty about a detail outside these dependencies can remain. A claim of physical impossibility needs its realized premises preserved by every extension it excludes, as M71 proves. A proposed interpretation can remain open without being counted as such a prohibition.

For the additive comparisons of S25, suppose each addressed offset obeys a justified bound |δᵢ−δ̂ᵢ|≤εᵢ in the same rate unit. For the loop sums L=Σδᵢ and L̂=Σδ̂ᵢ, the triangle inequality gives |L−L̂|≤Σ εᵢ. Hence |L̂|>Σ εᵢ certifies L≠0. No statistical independence is assumed. If the resulting interval contains both zero and nonzero values, that bound alone decides neither exact equality nor its failure. The singleton interval {0} would certify equality. Sharper joint bounds or a structural identity can settle a question that the separate bounds leave open. Changing local clock origins cancels in the loop sum, so absolute origins need not be acquired for this question. This numerical argument remains conditional on the actual additive comparison and calibration relations. A more precise number cannot repair a wrong source binding or comparison model.

For the foundational chain, a useful question exposes a premise that several conclusions use differently. LQ16's self-contact, M55's proper record extension and M72's recovery boundary are such points. Whether a repeated local form carries a new occurrence changes the time claim. Whether an earlier distinction factors through a later complete cut changes the retention claim. Whether a proposed replacement preserves the actual contact changes the identification claim. Each can be tested by an addressed equality or recovery equation, with a positive difference supplying an obstruction. Resolving every microscopic field, fixing an absolute origin or selecting one canonical name is unnecessary when the requested conclusion is already the same throughout those remaining choices.

## §2b. A Past Distinction Crosses Every Cut

This section is an entry. It gathers laws of this paper, of Verdict and of Distance that decide when a past distinction remains answerable, and it adds no theorem of its own. Labels marked history refer to accepted research notes listed in §7.

**Question.** What must a whole retain so that a distinction made earlier can be answered later? Which records of the past can keep growing?

**Result.**
1. A later reading answers a question about an earlier occurrence exactly when the question factors through that reading on the declared compatible histories. Established recovery then requires a witness of the distinction at every complete cut in between.
2. The witness can be joint. A local reading can coincide on two histories while their joint relation still separates them.
3. Growth has conditions. A whole that returns completely carries no count of its returns. Under an invariant probability a reading that never decreases along the step is almost surely unchanged by it.

**Recovery is factorization.** Fix a family H of compatible complete histories, a question Q about an earlier occurrence and a reading E of a later cut. A decoder D recovers Q exactly when Q=D∘E on H (M50). Two histories with one E-image and different Q-answers leave no single-valued decoder. Let J join admitted prefixes p and continuations f inside one whole, and let a reading r of f recover q(p) on every joined pair. Two prefixes that share a continuation then have the same earlier answer, and different answers need disjoint continuation families (M72). Here every prefix has a nonempty continuation family N(p), and the cut is complete for J: (p,f)∈J exactly when (c(p),f)∈S for some relation S. Every complete cut between the occurrence and the reading then carries the distinction. Without nonempty families a constant cut of an empty relation could merge different answers.

**A joint witness.** The carrier of that recovery need not be one register. In a reversible exchange a local reading can miss a distinction that the joint state keeps. Under VC26a's conditions, a comparison has reciprocal differential dependence. Let the complete whole be a finite-dimensional nondegenerate symplectic product over the cut, with every controller, clock and memory on its assigned side. Let Φ(m,c)=(F(m,c),ψ(m,c)) be a symplectic map on an open product. Then D_cF=0 if and only if D_mψ=0 at each point. On connected open factors this gives equivalence of global independence. Dependence in one direction only across the full cut requires a failed premise, for example a nonproduct form or a prepared closed layer (G14d). Individual directed edges between smaller subsystems can be one-way. For a finite whole with a fixed residual alphabet, exact recovery of the whole from a compressed image needs at least as many residual values as the largest fiber of the image (M35).

**When a record can grow.** A complete recurrence carries no distinguishable lap count. A counter that separates the returns is a coordinate in which the whole has not returned (M51). Let U preserve a probability measure on the entire whole. A measurable reading V with V(Ux)≥V(x) almost surely then satisfies V(Ux)=V(x) almost surely (M36). The step leaves it unchanged outside a null set, while V itself can vary across the whole. Let U be a bimeasurable bijection preserving μ, and let Γ be measurable and forward invariant, with μ(Γ)<∞. Its entry cut B=Γ∖UΓ is null, because μ(UΓ)=μ(Γ) and UΓ⊆Γ (history SA49.4). The measure of the entire carrier need not be finite. Recoverability alone defines no strict temporal order. That order needs a proper extension of the record (M55).

**A residue is not a counter by name.** In the field model of the research history the full Source phase is an actual residue (history AD97b). Its formula can depend on the pass number n (history HPU77), and a nonzero resonance can return the same Source at the ends of different passes (history HOR45). That dependence alone does not make the phase an injective record of n, because the angle is cyclic and the contact times depend on the contents. An injective record must be exhibited with its carrier, access and update law.

**Scope of §2b.** These are conditions on what a record can certify. They do not supply a physical carrier, a reader or the energy of an update. A measure premise is a premise. A Hamiltonian field gives no finite invariant measure by its form alone.

## §3. The Transition

Reuse replaces some acquisition or derivation with retrieval and checking. In a sequential lookup model with K entries and F constant-cost features per entry, lookup uses at most K·F feature comparisons. The cost of checking, retrieving the features, building and maintaining the library must be added. An old derivation cost greater than this full bill gives a saving; the reverse inequality gives a loss.

Retained access is expressed by the same record supplying a later answer (VC42). No new source distinction is needed when the question already factors through that record. A runtime or energy advantage needs a separate cost interpretation (AI6, M27).

A retaining resolution map can stop changing when all its declared questions are covered. Its construction can include repeated unsuccessful work, and PASS need not add a fresh entry. This scoped fixed point is the sense of crystal used below. Continued activity of the whole need not alter that map.

## §4. Levels

A retained result can become the declared input or target of another game. The next game still needs its own candidates, question, allowed operations and controller. One whole contains all these roles. No external designer is required by the model, but internal closure alone does not construct a successor policy.

### Time

**M1 (An operation clock).** A sequential comparison model may index completed operations by natural numbers. This is its counting convention. It does not exclude continuous physical time or additional internal processes in an implementation.

**M2 (Trace-determined state).** Fix the initial state and deterministic update rule, and include every state-changing input in a complete operation trace. Replaying that trace gives the same final state independently of an omitted wall-clock label. This follows by induction on the trace. If timing affects the update, timing belongs in the inputs and cannot be omitted.

**M3 (Replay equivalence).** Two copies of that same initialized machine receiving the same complete trace have equal states at every corresponding step. Equal Boolean comparison results alone are insufficient: comparing different pairs can return `equal` while writing different cache keys. The operands and update data matter.

**M4 (Level-relative counts).** A declared macro-operation can contain several lower-level operations. A high-level tick counts completion of that macro, while lower-level work counts its constituent actions. Their dependencies need not form one chain. Distance G14a–G14b constructs compatible tree-edit rounds and separates the number of edits from the longest dependency chain using actual applications. Assigning physical durations or hardware resource limits requires a realization of that interface.

> Argues: ``Tower`` needs ``HasTime``.

**M5 (Conditional nesting bound).** Suppose each level-(n+1) operation uses at most b_n level-n operations and has no uncharged overhead in this counting unit. If each level-n operation costs at most T_n base operations, then T_(n+1) ≤ b_n T_n. With T_0=1, induction gives T_N ≤ ∏_(n<N) b_n. The number of task classes K_n is not a bound on retries or search, so replacing b_n by K_n needs an extra operation bound. This is an upper bound, not a proof that every higher level is strictly or exponentially slower.

> Argues: ``Tower`` needs ``HasCost``.

### Ignition

**M6 (Mutable target boundary).** A changing library that changes during a comparison sequence cannot be treated as one unchanged target without modeling those changes. An immutable snapshot can be a target before the changing library saturates. Incompleteness alone does not forbid targethood.

**M7 (A scoped stable result).** If the update rule adds only missing entries from a fixed finite declared set, complete coverage leaves no new entry to add. Stability follows for that update rule. It does not prove complete future-task coverage after a scope change, nor minimality of an encoding.

**M8 (A sufficient handoff).** A stable library can be supplied as the target of another game once its representation, candidate space and comparison procedure are declared. Saturation is one sufficient way to obtain stability under M7. It is not the unique instant at which a target becomes possible: a partial snapshot already supplies a counterexample to that earlier necessity claim.

> Argues: ``Ignites`` needs ``LibrarySaturates``.
> Argues: ``Ignites`` needs ``Matter``.

The `Ignites` intersection records a chosen architecture's saturation and matter premises. Its conformance does not execute a handoff, instantiate a controller or prove the absence of other constructions.

**M9 (An internal successor needs a mechanism).** The next game may be built and run entirely within the whole. Merely having a library, other structures and a comparison operation does not guarantee it will run: a system can retain the library and repeat one old query indefinitely. Automatic ignition requires an actual transition or scheduling premise.

**M10 (Iterated handoff).** When M8's interfaces and an internal controller are supplied at each step, one result can become another game's input. Iterating that construction is a model of a tower. A finite number of candidates at each level supplies no bound on the number of levels.

### Exhaustion and Form

**M11 (Novel-comparison bound).** On a fixed finite comparison domain D, at most |D|² ordered pairs can enter a faithful retaining cache for the first time. The total number of calls can be arbitrarily large: repeating one pair does not visit the others. Full coverage requires a schedule covering every pair. A finite discovery budget requires bounds on that schedule, not just on the number of possible records (M26).

**M12 (Stabilization of finite retaining records).** Let L_t and G_t grow by inclusion within fixed finite record sets. There are at most the sum of those sets' sizes strict additions; hence along an infinite trace the two records eventually stop changing. This follows because the finite set of actual addition times has a last member, if it is nonempty. It gives no uniform time to that last addition and no guarantee that unvisited records or unresolved questions are covered. Stabilized records do not make the whole state stationary; clocks and repeated operations can continue.

**M13 (Properties of a library need witnesses).** Multiple entries can support shared structure or a shorter dispatch tree. They need not do so, and such structure can exist before coverage is complete. For identifying K nonempty answer classes with binary queries, worst-case depth is at least ⌈log₂K⌉ by the leaf bound. Attaining that depth requires suitable queries. Uniqueness of each class's answer supplies no such queries (S3a, S5).

**M14 (Different descriptions can have different tasks).** A higher-level operation may ask about relations among entries instead of one entry. Which relations it can read and how long they take depend on its interface. Coarser descriptions and slower ticks are possible design choices, not consequences of the level number.

**M15 (Successor parameters require more than the library).** If a new interface is exactly a vector of K Boolean match outcomes, there are at most 2^K such vectors. Actual distinguishability, available queries and costs require their own specification. Under IID uniform draws over K′ classes, complete acquisition from scratch has mean K′H_(K′) draws. The library alone establishes neither that sampling law nor K′<K. A binary decision tree for K′ classes again obeys M13's lower bound.

**M16 (Conditional tower termination).** If a declared integer rank K_n strictly decreases whenever K_n>1 and construction stops at K_n≤1, it stops after at most K_0−1 decreases for K_0≥1. Neither strict descent nor the stopping rule follows from closure, finite individual games or the existence of compression. This rank argument does not prove a globally shortest representation of the original domain.

**M17 (A constructed tree boundary).** Every finite tree constructed by the declared PAIR/NULL grammar has terminating paths by structural induction. This claim is about those expressions. Arbitrary recursive type conformances can contain cycles, and the type named NULL does not remove the need to state the construction grammar.

### Protocol

**M18 (A library with a lookup procedure).** A fixed library and a fixed deterministic lookup procedure determine a trace from the full input. Different inputs can traverse different branches. The library's tree alone does not select a unique procedure. All lookups are internal operations of the whole.

**M19 (Resistance during a declared comparison).** In the comparison model, mismatch is encountered at a comparison event. The chosen operation count can assign zero comparison work between events. That convention proves nothing about passive physical maintenance, other internal dynamics or existence of matter between comparisons.

**M20 (Reuse preserves a scoped answer).** A stored conditional result can be reapplied when its assumptions, question and representation remain valid. Restricting a nonempty compatible family preserves a common answer; widening it may require a new proof (CS3, VC35–VC36, AI6). Repeating a successful lookup is consistent with the certificate, but is not independent evidence that an assumed model describes the source. Memory integrity and access are additional implementation obligations.

**M21 (Retention is a declared update property).** For a fixed fact vocabulary and decoding, a retaining cache update satisfies G_t⊆G_(t+1). I3 imposes this relation on the permitted record trajectories. Truth of a pure comparison alone does not implement accessible storage (System GC1–GC3). The record update and its faithful interpretation must be supplied.

This restriction on forward cache updates does not forbid an inverse for the complete physical evolution or recovery of a prior record. An append retains its predecessor as a component (M29), and the closed examples in M30–M37 preserve the entire input while changing local access. The protocol `NoInverse` constrains `Library: Permanent`. It does not express a theorem that the complete whole has no inverse map.

**M22 (Lifetime follows the retention premise).** If a certified fact is accessible at time t and every subsequent permitted step retains it under the same faithful decoding and access conditions, induction preserves it at every later step. This is a conditional statement about those updates, not a physical proof of unlimited record lifetime after finite work.

A physical realization must account for its controls, storage and permitted reading window. An indefinitely retained newly acquired record does not follow from injectivity, a stable truth value, or finite work alone (M33, M36–M37). Likewise, an ignition state persists only while the conditions that define it persist. Mathematical recovery of the past and persistent access to the record are separate properties.

**M23 (Cumulative capacity and overlap).** Faithful retention across N completed domains with disjoint scoped record keys gives |L|=Σ_i K_i when domain i adds exactly K_i entries. This is finite for finite N and finite K_i. Unbounded growth additionally requires unboundedly many distinct additions and adequate storage; persistence alone does not provide them.

If m of K target classes are already correctly covered in the same scope, IID uniform draws over those K classes take mean K·H_(K−m) draws to see all remaining classes. At j unseen classes, the chance of a new class is j/K, so the expected waiting time is K/j; summing proves the formula. Semantic overlap must be established, not inferred from similar labels. The formula counts acquisition draws, not dispatch, checking or maintenance cost. A logarithmic lookup improvement requires the query structure of M13. Accumulation can help, remain neutral or become expensive under different resource accounts.

### Cost

**M24 (Physical cost requires an implementation).** I3 retains comparison records. It supplies neither a probability distribution nor an energy scale. A new record can be a reversible correlation between parts of one closed system. Recording, changing a tree node, and resetting an unknown bit are different operations. [Bennett's reversible computation construction](https://www.cs.princeton.edu/courses/archive/fall06/cos576/papers/bennett73.html) supplies an explicit counterexample to a mandatory dissipation charge for every logical step. Its clean output has its own contract. The construction computes, copies the result into a prepared blank register and uncomputes. Reversibility alone does not need that blank: the permutation (x,y)↦(x,y⊕f(x)) is invertible for every y. In that construction the blank is needed to deliver f(x) itself in a declared output register beside the retained input x, and the price of preparing it depends on the declared architecture. Verdict VC59 counts one such slot account and states that its count is not a universal price of a bit.

Under initially uncorrelated memory and thermal reservoir at positive temperature and joint unitary evolution, Landauer bounds reservoir heat by the memory's entropy decrease: `Q ≥ k_B T ΔS`, with entropy in nats. Resetting an unbiased classical bit gives `ΔS = ln 2`. Finite reservoirs add restrictions. Both memory and reservoir are internal parts of the whole. This is a lower bound, not an upper budget or a price for passive storage. See [Reeb and Wolf, Theorem 3](https://arxiv.org/html/1306.4352).

A maintenance model may assign a measured or assumed cost `c` per stored node per correction cycle. Then its bill is `c × Σ_entry F_entry`, or `c × |L| × F` for equal entry sizes. Neither uniform pricing nor `c = k_B T ln 2` follows from I1–I3. Retained correlations and the implementation of error correction must be included when applying a physical bound.

**M25 (Conditional cost management).** Under the bisecting-predicate premise of M15, a decision tree can reduce comparison depth from linear to logarithmic in the number of classes. An energy improvement additionally requires a resource model for storage, access, construction and correction. Shorter lookup does not by itself reduce the retained library or its maintenance cost. Finite storage at each moment is compatible with unbounded capacity over time, but a fixed finite device cannot store an unbounded independent record.

**When does preservation require a distinguishing response?** Having a cost does not itself require a cost report. Let T⊆X×M relate possible source conditions to complete response inputs. Let V⊆X×A specify which responses preserve the stipulated requirement in each condition. Restrict M to inputs related to at least one source. A response relation R⊆M×A is universally adequate precisely when

    T(x,m) and R(m,a) imply V(x,a).

The largest such relation is

    R_max(m) = ⋂_{x:T(x,m)} {a∈A : V(x,a)}.

*Proof.* Any admitted response at m must be allowed for every source related to m, so it belongs to that intersection. Conversely every member of the intersection meets every one of those requirements. Thus a nonempty response set can be supplied at each admitted input exactly when every displayed intersection is nonempty. R_max itself supplies those sets, with no choice of representatives. It does not select or implement an individual response. This is a condition for every admitted contact to preserve the requirement, not a statement about a probability-one guarantee. Indefinitely repeatable preservation must also cover the successor contacts.

The factorization through m is substantive. All source/response combinations allowed by T and R are being covered. If a mechanism correlates its response with x through another internal relation, that relation belongs to its complete input or to a more detailed joint contact. Equal reported symbols alone do not remove such a dependence. Neither this test nor its failure requires a separate sensor register, an external controller or a felt cost.

If one response a₀ is allowed in every source condition, the constant response suffices for this requirement despite ignorance of the actual condition. If two conditions require different unique responses, one common complete input cannot support both. More generally a whole family can conflict while each pair shares an answer: response sets {p,q}, {q,r} and {p,r} meet pairwise but have empty total intersection. A successful realization of this contract must avoid combining that conflicting family at one response input, change an admitted response or abandon the requirement. Mere survival in an unspecified range does not establish that such a conflict exists.

This is the same structure as LQ15. Each response a has its source region B_a={x:V(x,a)}. The nonempty source families admitting a common response are exactly those contained in some B_a. Forgetting the response address leaves a downward-closed family of compatible source sets. Its two-place projection need not retain the joint refusal. No new algebra is needed. When V(x,a) means a=q(x), the criterion reduces to the exact recovery condition of Verdict §6c. Preservation can require less than recovering the source or even one designated source value.

When a reading assigns one code to each source, there need not be one coarsest adequate reading. Let conditions x,y,z admit responses {a}, {a,b}, {b}, respectively. One adequate reading merges x with y and separates z. Another merges y with z and separates x. A reading coarser than both must merge all three and would require one response in {a}∩{a,b}∩{b}, which is empty. Both two-answer readings are minimal, yet neither replaces the other. Exact recovery of a stipulated q has its canonical answer fibers. Preserving a relation of acceptable responses instead permits different adequate ways of distinguishing the conditions. The ambiguity belongs to that task, not to an incomplete search for its unique quotient.

**When is there one coarsest adequate reading?** Write V_x={a:V(x,a)} and assume each V_x is nonempty. Connect two source conditions when they share an allowed response. The connected components use finite paths of these contacts, even when X is infinite. A coarsest adequate partition of X exists exactly when every such component C admits a common response:

    ⋂_{x∈C} V_x ≠ ∅.

When it exists, that component partition is the answer. Indeed, any adequate block lies within one component. Conversely, each pair sharing an allowed response can be merged by an adequate partition that leaves every other source as a singleton. A partition coarser than every adequate partition must therefore merge every component. Such a merger is adequate precisely under the displayed condition. This proves necessity and sufficiency without choosing a response for each component: the intersection supplies the adequate response relation. Implementing a selection is another requirement. LQ15's finite overlap criterion describes the same obstruction through jointly admitted source families. On an infinite component, admitting every finite subfamily alone need not admit the whole.

The component criterion is weaker than equality of full contact profiles. The rows {a} and {a,b} can share one adequate code by retaining response a, although only the second permits b. LQ13's exact contact boundary must distinguish those rows. The same relation can thus have different sufficient descriptions for choosing one permitted response and for preserving every contact answer.

**M25a (A future restriction can force an order of response profiles).** Fix a nonempty family S of possible source conditions at one complete input. Let I_S=⋂_{x∈S}V_x. A later internal contact restricts the response to K⊆A, without reading the hidden x. Ask whether the following implication is valid for every K:

    [for every x∈S, V_x∩K ≠ ∅]  implies  I_S∩K ≠ ∅.

The left side says each possible actual source has some permitted response. The right side says one response works without another distinction of the source. This implication is valid for all K exactly when one source x₀∈S has V_(x₀)=I_S.

*Proof.* Such a least row supplies the result immediately: the left premise applied to x₀ gives a member of I_S∩K. If there is no such row, every V_x contains an element outside I_S. Take K=A\I_S. Each source then has a permitted response, while no response is common. The proof works for arbitrary sets and needs no distribution or finite enumeration. QED

For example, let V_x={a,b} and V_y={b,c}. Before the contact, b serves both. The restriction K={a,c} leaves response a for x and c for y, but no response for their unchanged common input. It does not make the actual state choose whichever alternative helps. Further source information, a different response contact, or a changed requirement is needed. This is the quantifier distinction between ∀x∃a and ∃a∀x expressed by one finite obstruction.

An autonomous coupling may respond with a at x and c at y without a separate sensor or state estimate. Such a coupling retains a source-dependent relation that the common-input factorization omitted. M25a does not prohibit it or require consciousness. It prohibits claiming both that the complete response input is unchanged and that an unrepresented dependence supplies its choice.

Now require the same implication after every nonempty source refinement T⊆S and every response restriction K. Applying the theorem to each T shows that this stronger property is satisfied exactly when every nonempty subfamily of the distinct rows {V_x:x∈S} has a least member under inclusion. Equivalently, those distinct rows are well-ordered by inclusion. For a finite row family, they must form a chain: any two rows are nested. No numerical rank or temporal traversal was assumed. This order is forced by the stipulated uniform-response requirement under those contacts. It is not a derivation that all physical interactions or all VI relations are linearly ordered.

Incomparable rows have a small positive obstruction: responses a∈V_x\V_y and b∈V_y\V_x. The two-source restriction {x,y} and two-response restriction {a,b} suffice to exhibit the failure. Excluding every such crossed pattern is exactly the familiar Ferrers condition

    V(x,a) and V(y,b) imply V(x,b) or V(y,a).

Its equivalence to nested profiles is immediate: a violation supplies one member of each row outside the other, and conversely. The relation-algebra formulation is established in [Backhouse and Voermans, §7, Lemma 120](https://arxiv.org/html/2401.17130v1#S7). LQ13's three-corner condition instead says overlapping profiles coincide. A chain and an equality interface are different possible structures extracted from contacts.

**Mutual self-contact makes the restriction sharper.** Suppose source and response roles use the same carrier, V is symmetric, and every x admits contact with itself. Take S to be that complete carrier. If x and y cannot contact one another, the same two-source, two-response example is forced by their self-contacts: each can use itself, neither can use the other. Thus a symmetric reflexive contact satisfies the universal restriction-and-refinement promise only when every pair is admitted. One actual refused pair defeats that promise. This concerns the specified adequacy relation. The mere fact that two physical forms interact need not mean they can jointly realize the requested response, and physical source/response roles need not be symmetric. No observer or numerical geometry is required for this obstruction.

Both quantifiers matter. With rows {a}, {a,b}, {a,c}, the whole family has a least row, but removing the first leaves two incomparable rows. In an infinite chain V_n={m∈ℕ:m≥n}, every finite subfamily has a least row. The complete family has none, and K=ℕ exposes the failure. Restricting the available contact grammar can weaken these conditions. Adding true source information never removes an already common response for a fixed K. What can fail after refinement is the stronger promise that individual feasibility always supplies a common response.

`CommonCandidateResponse<C,P,Q>` binds two actual selection sources to the two catalogues in C and requires their selected responses to agree. In the finite leaf/fork and recursive-join grammar, it supplies a common-response witness. The three-response construction in `Checks/Interactions.swift` admits b for both original sources and admits a and c separately after the same new contact. It refuses both actual menu entries as substitutes for b, and also refuses inventing a b leaf whose catalogue is not that menu. `ResponseLiftsThroughInclusion` proves generically that a response supported by a least catalogue and the contact remains supported when the former catalogue is included in a larger one. The universal set criteria above are mathematical proofs. These type constructions establish the addressed finite witnesses and inclusion rule.

**What remains canonical when one adequate partition does not exist?** For S⊆X put F(S)=⋂_{x∈S}V_x, with F(∅)=A, and for K⊆A put G(K)={x:K⊆V_x}. Their defining relation is

    K⊆F(S)  iff  S⊆G(K).

Hence C(S)=G(F(S)) contains S, preserves F(S), and satisfies C(C(S))=C(S). It is the greatest source family with that same common-response set: F(T)=F(S) implies T⊆C(S). The pair (C(S),F(S)) is therefore fixed by the given contact relation, even when its source regions overlap. This is the established Galois closure of [formal concept analysis, commonality operators and Galois connection](https://lat.inf.tu-dresden.de/~francesco/KR2023-tutorial-session1.pdf), applied here to permitted responses. It needs no choice of a partition or an optimizer. Source existence must be retained separately: the empty family has every response only vacuously.

This compression has an exact composition scope. Adding alternative source conditions obeys F(S∪T)=F(S)∩F(T), so the two common-response sets determine the new one. A response restriction similarly gives F(S)∩K. Restricting the possible sources by new evidence need not descend to this image. Let V_x={a,b}, V_y={a}, V_z={a,c}. The families S₁={x,y} and S₂={y,z} both have image {a} and closure {x,y,z}. The same later evidence E={x,z} leaves two nonempty families, {x} and {z}, whose response sets differ. Neither the old common-response image nor its Galois closure can determine that update. The joint relation with the new evidence was omitted.

Thus a canonical description for current guaranteed responses need not support continued discovery. To promise later refinement, retain enough source/contact correspondence for those refinements, as VC15 requires. In the extreme grammar containing every singleton source test, preservation of feasibility after all such tests already determines the full source support. Seam §3 gives the boundary-restricted version. These are questions about the information retained at a given interface. They do not imply that omitted distinctions disappeared from the whole.

For a fixed finite response alphabet A of size r, an empty intersection has a witness using at most r source conditions: for each response a, choose one related source x_a that refuses it. Those finitely many sources already exclude every response. The bound is sharp: with A={1,…,r} and allowed sets A\{i}, all r conditions together conflict and every proper subfamily admits a response. This bounds a refusal certificate without requiring a finite whole or finite source family. New source conditions cannot repair that refusal. New responses can: completeness of the response alphabet remains a premise.

With an infinite response alphabet that finite witness need not exist. Take A=ℕ and one common input for source conditions n∈ℕ, with response a allowed exactly when a≥n. Every finite family admits a common response, while the whole family admits none. The finite action bound, not closure of reality, was doing the work. This is the same finite-domain issue examined in VC23. It is not a general proof that finite evidence settles every impossibility.

This is a qualitative regulation criterion, not a new general theory of control. [Ashby's *An Introduction to Cybernetics*, §10/7 and Chapter 11](https://ashby.info/Ashby-Introduction-to-Cybernetics.pdf) already distinguishes passive protection from informed counteraction and derives regulation bounds under explicit response-table assumptions. Here V must come from the particular preservation question and its established contacts. Calling V survival or cost supplies no such contacts. The Cut §13's cost-report model therefore cannot derive its signals, or feeling, from maintenance expenditure alone.

**A finite refusal also bounds probabilistic success.** Fix an input m and one response law μ used for every source condition related to m. All response sets mentioned here are measurable, and μ is a countably additive probability on A. Write B_x=A\V_x for the responses refused in condition x. If r source conditions have no common allowed response, their refusal sets cover A. Therefore

    ∑_(i=1)^r μ(B_(x_i)) ≥ μ(A) = 1,
    max_(1≤i≤r) μ(B_(x_i)) ≥ 1/r.

This follows by integrating the fact that every answer is refused by at least one of the r conditions. The bound is sharp for A={1,…,r}, B_i={i} and uniform μ. If every answer is refused by at least k of those conditions, the same argument gives k/r. More generally, nonnegative weights w_i satisfying ∑_i w_i 1_(B_(x_i))(a)≥1 for every a yield ∑_i w_i μ(B_(x_i))≥1. The obstruction is in the joint coverage, without an entropy or dynamics premise.

The common law is part of the contract. A source-dependent response kernel or a seed correlated with the source may change the attainable errors. That dependence must be included in the joint description. The sources, seed and preparation all remain internal. No objective randomness or exterior sampler follows from this mathematical account of uncertainty.

**Probability one for each condition need not give one common safe set.** Compare

    for every x, μ(V_x)=1

with the existence of a measurable Z of probability one such that Z⊆V_x for every x. The second implies the first. The first implies the second for a finite or countable condition family: its countable intersection has probability one. This uses the same countable-null-union fact as PP5c. Even that common set can exclude admitted answers of measure zero, so it is weaker than requiring every admitted response to be safe.

For an uncountable family, probability one in each condition need not supply a common Z. Let X=A=[0,1], V_x=[0,1]\{x}, and let μ be uniform on the interval. Every fixed condition has μ(V_x)=1, but ⋂_x V_x is empty. Each answer fails in the condition bearing its own value. Choosing that condition after seeing the answer introduces a dependence absent from a fixed condition and common response law. The two success questions have different quantifiers. This is a measure-theoretic counterdescription, without a physical device that produces exact real-valued answers.

The same diagonal-refusal example distinguishes three limits. For X=A and B_x={x}, let e(μ)=sup_x μ({x}):

| Response domain and admitted conditions | Infimum of worst-condition error and attainment |
|---|---|
| r distinct values | 1/r, attained by the uniform law |
| ℕ | Infimum 0, attained by no probability law |
| [0,1] with its usual measurable sets | 0, attained by the uniform law |

For ℕ, a law uniform on the first N answers has e=1/N. Yet e=0 would assign zero mass to every singleton and hence, by countable additivity, to all of ℕ. For the interval, each singleton already has measure zero under the uniform law. Thus arbitrarily small worst-condition error, an attained probability-one guarantee for each condition, and a universally allowed answer are distinct requirements. Cardinality here belongs to the declared response example, not to a conclusion about the whole of reality.

**What extra bridge lets probability zero exclude a contact?** Suppose every admitted bad answer has a witness w whose entire compatible-answer class C_w is bad. If every such admitted witness class has μ(C_w)>0, a nonempty bad set has positive measure: choose any of its answers and then its witness. Consequently μ(B_x)=0 implies B_x is empty. A finite or countable answer domain with positive mass at every admitted answer supplies one instance, regardless of how many source conditions are considered.

A different instance permits infinite answers. Suppose each refusal is certified by finitely many addressed readings and remains a refusal under every admitted extension of those readings. If every nonempty class specified by such a finite joint record has positive preparation probability, the same proof applies. Under the IID fair-bit law on infinite binary sequences, every length-n prefix has probability 2⁻ⁿ. A refusal forced by some prefix therefore cannot have probability zero. Equality to one specified infinite sequence is different: its singleton has probability at most 2⁻ⁿ for every n and hence zero, while no finite prefix by itself certifies that equality.

The positivity requirement concerns the joint witness class. Positive probabilities for its fields separately do not establish it. Nor does a finite written formula necessarily specify a positive-probability class: a short rule can describe a single infinite sequence. M71 supplies finite refusal chains for its identity contract, but it assigns no probabilities to their source classes. This bridge needs both an actual stable refusal witness and a preparation giving its class positive mass. Sampling without an established coverage or structural argument does not certify these premises. These arguments use ordinary countable additivity and finite covering. Their purpose is to delimit the probability reading of the already specified contacts.

**M26 (Novel-comparison bound).** For a fixed finite comparison domain `D ⊂ Σ` with persistent caching, at most `|D|²` ordered pairs can receive their first result. This bounds uncached comparisons, including uncached refusals. Repeated queries can continue without bound, and a cached refusal remains a refusal. The bound concerns new comparison work, not the disappearance of structural distance.

If an implementation supplies an upper cost `ε` for each such comparison and a total overhead budget `H`, then its discovery expenditure is at most `|D|² ε + H`. Landauer supplies neither upper bound.

**M27 (Cost transformation).** Crystallization replaces some repeated derivation with lookup and retained records. Whether this lowers total expenditure depends on task count, lookup cost, record size and maintenance duration. A break-even calculation must charge construction and storage as well as the saved comparisons. Structural reuse alone does not prove an unconditional decrease in joules per task.

**Theorem M28 (Conditional finite discovery budget).** Suppose the tower has finitely many levels, each level has a certified finite operation bound `N_n`, each charged operation has a finite upper cost `ε_n`, and total overhead has a finite upper bound `H`. Then discovery expenditure is bounded by `Σ_n N_n ε_n + H`.

*Proof.* Sum the supplied upper bounds over the finitely many operations and levels. QED

M16 provides tower termination only under its compression premise. Formal comparison counts must be translated into implementation operations to provide `N_n`. A minimum heat per reset cannot provide `ε_n`. This theorem bounds a completed discovery computation under those budgets, not the cost of preserving its record for infinite time. The current `FiniteDiscovery` protocol does not encode the physical upper budgets and does not certify them.

## §4a. The closed whole, records and entropy

Take Ω to be the entire system. There is no external observer, memory, sink or controller. A partition into A and B names internal parts. Every physical implementation must include its controls and storage within Ω. The following claims separate conditions that were previously compressed into the word permanence.

The Cut's record commitment concerns the full history. Its continued growth does not imply that every new event changes an answer already settled in a fixed scope. VI's finite saturation is compatible with that commitment: an interface can finish acquiring its required distinctions without enumerating every later event. Conversely, our finite reversible cycles describe prepared acquisition windows, not an implementation of unbounded historical storage.

The chain from one whole to usable knowledge therefore needs internal reading, a separating interaction, a retained record and a compatible subsequent question. `PreparedFirstAnswerIsCertified` checks the symbolic reading of a prepared transition's output. `AcquireAnswer` and `AnswerInTrace` express recording and retained access as relations (VC40–VC42). VC38 supplies its dynamic compression criterion: states may share an interface only when every permitted continuation preserves the relevant common answers. A discarded interface distinction still belongs to the whole.

**M29 (Closure and preservation).** For a deterministic complete-state model with carrier K, closure says `T: K → K`. Preservation of all input distinctions says `T(x) = T(y) ⇒ x = y`. Closure alone does not entail preservation: the two-state constant map is closed and merges its inputs. A recovery map satisfying `R(T(x)) = x` proves preservation by applying R to an assumed output equality. Thus the no-loss conception requires this stronger relation, or an equivalent no-merger condition.

The closure tested here is closure of the declared state carrier under its transition. If the whole instead denotes all actual events and their relations, including their historical positions, a current-state projection need not be that whole. A merger of projected states then leaves open whether the histories differ. M29 does not refute retention in that larger object; M50 states the additional bridge to a later reading.

The existing `Permanent` protocol already contains a recovery witness: its post-record `Right` has `Right.Left == Left`, the previous record. `RetainedWhole<Before, Event>` constructs that relation for arbitrary inputs. This is an explicit structural model of retention, not a derivation of physical reversibility from the word closed. An append constructor is invertible on its image, without being a permutation of a fixed finite carrier.

**M30 (Internal reset and internal recording).** Consider two classical bits within Ω. On the support `(x, 0)`, SWAP gives `(0, x)`: A resets while the whole retains x. CNOT gives `(x, x)`: B records A. Both operations extend to permutations of all four states. With uniform x the joint Shannon entropy is one bit before and after. After copying, each marginal has one bit and their mutual information is one bit. A record creates a correlation, not a second independent bit. Preparing a blank register is a resource condition, and these logical tables assert no physical energy cost.

**M31 (Retention can be relational).** Let x and r be independent uniform bits inside Ω. The permutation `(x, r) → (r, x XOR r)` retains x in the joint output. Neither output alone has any mutual information with x. Joint XOR recovers it. Information need not move into an individually readable component when it disappears from another component. Preservation establishes recoverability under a specified decoder, not that any internal process actually runs that decoder or can implement it with available resources.

The admitted input family matters. IiS12 separates this classical message from a common isometric encoding that makes one complete quantum marginal independent of every coherent input. In that stronger contract the complementary part must recover the input. A phase family can still be hidden from both individual outputs while retained jointly, as the existing IiS16 construction shows. Its local readings cease to be constant when the omitted population preparations are admitted. Memory therefore requires a specified source question, partition and preparation family before one can decide which retained relations are sufficient.

**M32 (Which entropy).** For a classical distribution transported by a permutation, joint Shannon entropy is unchanged: its probabilities are reordered. In a quantum model with unitary whole-system evolution, the eigenvalues of the joint density operator are unchanged, so its von Neumann entropy is unchanged. Marginal entropies can change as correlations change. For the classical partitions above, `H(A,B) = H(A) + H(B) − I(A:B)`. The choice of distribution, observables and internal partition must be explicit. Tree size and number of historical events are not these entropies. A point distribution has zero Shannon entropy even when its one supported state has internal structure. The distribution describes possible configurations mathematically, without adding an observer or reservoir outside Ω.

A coarse observation can fail to recover a preserved distinction. This alone establishes neither a thermodynamic arrow nor monotonic entropy growth. Calling all entropy dilution or information destruction would erase the distinction between these mathematical quantities. A physical account of an arrow also needs dynamics, initial conditions and a specified macroscopic description.

**A relation can change its order without changing these entropies.** Prepare three internal classical bits uniformly on the four states satisfying a XOR b XOR c=0. Every individual bit is uniform, and every pair has the uniform four-outcome distribution. Nevertheless the joint has four possible states rather than eight. The reversible operation

```
T(a,b,c) = (a, b XOR c, c)
```

is its own inverse on all eight states. On the prepared support it gives (a,a,c), with a and c independent and uniform. The first two addressed bits now agree in every admitted state. Every individual entropy remains 1 bit and whole entropy remains 2 bits. The total correlation, defined here as H(A)+H(B)+H(C)−H(A,B,C), remains 1 bit. Initially that restriction is invisible to every pair marginal. Afterwards the first pair carries it as agreement. There is no need to locate the old constraint in one extra particle or to call its conversion a decrease of whole entropy.

The odd-parity preparation supplies the attack on an incomplete interface. It has exactly the same three pair marginals as the even preparation. Under the same T it gives (a,1 XOR a,c), so the first two bits disagree instead. All old pair marginals together therefore fail to determine this later agreement question. This is an instance of VC15's continuation requirement: the omitted three-way distinction becomes necessary under an admitted operation. Pairwise statistical independence is compatible with a joint constraint. Representing that constraint by nested `Pair` constructors does not make it a product of pairwise statistical constraints.

`ExchangeParityWeights` computes T's permutation of eight addressed weights. Its generic return equation recovers every weight. `ThreeBitMarginalOperands` regroups actual source entries for the three marginal sums. The existing `AddZero` / `AddSuccessor` and `AddNaturalMatrices` constructions give identical old marginals for the even and odd unit-weight preparations, then diagonal and off-diagonal first-pair tables after T. Each arithmetic input is bound to the actual regrouped weights. Positive zero/successor distinctions separate both the original joints and their later tables. Refusals reject identifying the whole from its pair marginals or predicting the same later table. Dividing the weights by four supplies the stated probabilities in this argument. The Swift relations establish the finite entries and recovery, without a probability runtime or a claim to realize the physical bits.

AC7 now characterizes this omission for a finite bipartite support: complete marginals determine every joint law exactly when the support graph is a forest. A particular response expectation needs less: its alternating sum must vanish on every cycle. These statements concern one declared family of joint alternatives. A single measurement basis need not supply such a complete family for further physical questions.

For example, in the two-qubit model, Ψ₊=(HV+VH)/√2 and Ψ₋=(HV−VH)/√2 have identical reduced density matrices I/2 and identical joint H/V probabilities. Yet the common observable X⊗X has answers +1 and −1, because it exchanges HV with VH. Thus even that entire joint H/V table cannot support both answers through one fixed response rule. The retained source must distinguish their relative coherence. This is a direct consequence of the stated quantum model, not a failure of the finite marginal criterion or a derivation of the quantum carrier from closure.

[Mattle et al.](https://courses.grainger.illinois.edu/phys513/sp2019/reading/week9/Dense_coding_Zeilinger1996.pdf) supply an experimental contact: changing one photon's polarization phase changed the two-photon coincidence pattern at a common analyzer. Their device distinguished three message classes, leaving two of the four Bell states unresolved. At matched paths, the reported identification reliabilities for Ψ₊ and Ψ₋ were 95% and 93%, conditional on detecting both photons. These measured joint responses support the relevance of the preparation relation. Exact equality of all local statistics above belongs to the ideal Bell-state calculation, not an assertion of exact experimental preparation or detection.

**An unchanged local environment can still change its relations.** Suppose a finite classical joint SC evolves by a permutation and the marginal distribution of C returns exactly. Subtracting H(S,C)=H(S)+H(C)−I(S:C) before and after gives ΔH(S)=ΔI(S:C). If the input is a product, local entropy can only increase, by the amount of newly created mutual information. With earlier correlations it can instead decrease as those correlations are consumed. The bit transformation (s,c)↦(s XOR c,c) exhibits both directions: (0,c) becomes (c,c), and (c,c) becomes (0,c), for uniform c. The environment value itself is unchanged in every case, but its relation to S changes.

This identity is also the accounting behind [Wilming's reversible catalysis result](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.05573). The paper's catalyst returns in its marginal state while correlations are permitted. Local return therefore does not certify return of the joint preparation. Reusing one fair internal bit to fill several prepared zero slots gives repeated copies of that same bit, each fair, with only one bit of joint entropy. A contract promising one fair output is weaker than a contract promising independent outputs across uses. The independence question needs its own joint relation. All registers and preparations in these examples are internal. No heat or work scale follows from the entropy identity.

Interference Is Substitution IiS12 supplies an explicit quantum instance. For two internally calibrated arms, an environment overlap g multiplies the cross-arm coherence. A balanced input gives local eigenvalues (1±|g|)/2, while its return probability to the prepared probe is (1+Re g)/2. Relative phase can therefore reverse the comparison answer while preserving purity and zero local von Neumann entropy. Orthogonal environment records instead yield one bit of local entropy within a pure joint state. Entropy and agreement with the selected reference are different questions about the same construction. IiS11–IiS13 identify the additional probe relations that constrain this freedom, with exact and bounded-error premises.

IiS14 gives a further boundary: transposition preserves the spectrum and entropy of every isolated density operator, but its action on one factor of a Bell pair produces a negative measurement weight. Entropy preservation therefore does not establish admissibility of a local rule on joint states. The required joint context is internal to the whole, with its own retained relations.

IiS19 also identifies a reversible reduced description. A finite-dimensional space of questions closed under an admitted unitary step carries an orthogonal update of its exact expectation coordinates. Those coordinates need not determine the whole density operator, but their distinctions cannot merge under that step. This supplies a criterion for lossless reuse relative to future questions even on an infinite state set. It concerns statistical descriptions and does not by itself make those statistics available from a single system or assign them a new entropy.

IiS15 supplies a positive construction with retained factors. The two vectors (1,0) and (2,3) generate the matrix with rows (5,6) and (6,9), whose entries and source relations are computed statically. After division by 14 this is a mixed local state, realized mathematically by a pure joint vector with two orthogonal internal record states. Its positive local entropy reflects the omitted joint relations. The description record in Swift preserves both factor vectors, without claiming to instantiate that quantum preparation.

IiS16 also ties the operator normalization, prepared record, joint action and two readings to one type-level source. All four input matrix entries are recovered from the joint output, while the local reading retains only the diagonal. The controlled-NOT realization supplies a reversible operation on a fixed internal system with a prepared memory part. The entropy distinction follows from the explicit preparation and reduction, without making literal unbounded history retention a consequence of this finite model.

The experimental comparison in IiS12 adds a representation boundary. A pseudopure encoding commutes with every unitary update of its fixed carrier and gives calibrated expectation values. It does not preserve tensor products of separately encoded states: global and separate preparation can have equal marginals and different joint responses. Thus transport of the selected dynamics does not by itself transport independence, purity or the entropy of the represented object. The preparation relation is part of the correspondence. Conversely, IiS12's full quantum contract fixes all unitaries, independent products and the factor reading. Those relations force identity or complete replacement on the source carrier, without assuming linearity of the representation. One preserved contrast selects identity. For a qubit, IiS12 obtains the same conclusion from H, T, CNOT, products and both marginal readings, with no assumed continuity. Omitting T admits a nonlinear fifth-power Pauli representation that preserves all the other relations. The stated quantum carrier and available joint comparisons carry these conclusions.

System IQ1–IQ3 separates another pair of orders. Exact evidence can shrink the set of possible sources while increasing posterior Shannon entropy on one rare branch. Conditional entropy decreases on average over the readings, not necessarily on each realized branch. A twelve-state reversible recording model exhibits this with source, memory and readiness all internal. The same analysis separates an answer's entropy from the cost and extra distinctions of the available questions. These are statistical and operational claims, not a thermodynamic arrow derived from the size of a candidate set.

**M33 (Complete history and instantaneous state).** An append-only history Ω_history records prior occurrences by construction. A complete instantaneous state Γ_t preserves their distinguishability only if the evolution and encoding do so. Writing a new event named reset does not prove that its record distinguishes the reset inputs. These are separate claims even when both descriptions concern the same whole.

On a fixed finite carrier an injective endomap is a permutation and every trajectory is periodic. A strictly increasing, literally retained record cannot be embedded into that same finite state space indefinitely. A finite record at each time with unbounded possible size is a different model and agrees with M23's finite-at-each-moment wording. Reversibility permits recurrence, while an indefinitely growing independent record requires capacity beyond a fixed finite bound.

Verdict Is Invariant VC32–VC34 makes this boundary constructive. An internal controller can acquire a correct answer during a prepared finite experiment, while a finite reversible whole retains every original distinction. The whole subsequently returns to its prepared configuration. A forward-invariant set of a finite permutation has no incoming transitions from its complement, so newly acquired readiness cannot become permanent under those assumptions. Recovery of prior distinctions and perpetual readability of a newly acquired answer therefore require different claims. Injectivity alone does not imply the literal append-only record required by I3.

**M34 (Discovery inside the whole).** In a fixed deterministic model with preserved global distinctions, discovery can be an internal change of correlation: an agent's record becomes sufficient for a question about another part. The learned object is specified by the question and the whole's structure, while access to it changes. This gives an operational reading of discovery without requiring information to arrive from outside Ω. It does not establish that every question has a unique answer or an accessible finite certificate. Physical content requires a model linking the structural distinctions to observables, dynamics, probabilities and resource units.

**What preparation makes a new constraint attainable?** Let K be a nonempty finite complete-state domain, P its nonempty prepared support, and R the states satisfying the desired output relation. If every permutation of K is admitted, there is one operation T with T(P)⊆R exactly when |P|≤|R|. Necessity follows because T is injective on P. For sufficiency choose an injection P→R, then biject the unused input states with the unused output states. The two maps form a permutation of K. This is one operation for every possible prepared input, rather than a different unobserved choice for each input.

The relation can therefore become certain on a prepared support without reducing the number of complete alternatives. It cannot become certain on all K when R is a proper subset. The parity preparation in M32 and its agreement target both contain four of eight states, and the displayed T supplies the required permutation. Whole closure places the preparation and all available controls inside the system. It does not select that preparation or grant every permutation as an available interaction.

For a fixed probability law p and nonempty R with |R|=m, the largest achievable probability of R over all permutations is the sum of the m largest input probabilities. The preimage of R always has m states, giving the bound. Mapping the m largest-weight states onto R attains it. Certainty is thus equivalent to support(p) having at most m members. Entropy at most log₂m is necessary but insufficient: on four states, (1−3ε,ε,ε,ε) has arbitrarily small entropy as ε tends to zero, yet for every 0<ε<1/4 it has full support and cannot be placed with certainty into a two-state target. A scalar entropy does not specify the exact attainable constraint.

The admitted operation family is a second independent restriction. On K=ℤ/4ℤ allow only rotations x↦x+k. Let P={0,2} and R={0,1}. Their cardinalities coincide and every individual state can reach R, but no one rotation maps P into R: the images remain an opposite pair. For uniform P the best success probability is 1/2. Relative separation of the two possible inputs obstructs uniform preparation even though the action is transitive on individual states. The missing structure belongs to how an operation acts on alternatives together. It is not repaired by counting their individual reachable states. An internal feedback policy can change the question, but then its acquired distinction and retained controller state must enter the whole, as VC31–VC33 require.

**An exact factor of a surjective change cannot have a nontrivial idempotent update.** Let U:X→X be surjective and E:X→Y one fixed reading onto its declared image Y. Suppose one deterministic F:Y→Y describes the next answer at every source:

    E∘U = F∘E    on all X.

Then F is surjective. Given y∈Y, choose x with E(x)=y and a predecessor z with U(z)=x. The equation gives y=F(E(z)). Consequently, if F²=F, choose w with F(w)=y and obtain F(y)=F²(w)=F(w)=y. Thus F=id. The source set, image and fibers may all be infinite. Injectivity of U, probability and a time metric were not used. Surjectivity and the same complete factor equation are additional facts, not consequences of the absence of an exterior.

More generally, a uniform eventual law F^(m+p)=F^m, with m≥0 and p>0, forces F^p=id. Surjectivity of F^m allows its cancellation from that equality. A global finite settling bound, where p=1, therefore permits no change at all. When Y is finite, surjectivity alone makes F a permutation. These conclusions concern a deterministic factor on the entire carried domain, rather than a coarse relation or a prepared episode.

**Pointwise settling and uniform settling differ.** Take X=ℤ, U(n)=n+1, Y={…,−2,−1,0}, E(n)=min(n,0) and F(y)=min(y+1,0). U is bijective, E is onto, and E(U(n))=F(E(n)) at every n. Each y reaches zero after finitely many steps and remains there. F is surjective and noninjective, with unbounded settling times across Y. For every m, the input y=−m−1 has F^m(y)=−1 and F^(m+1)(y)=0, so no common finite settling bound applies. This explicit counterconstruction prevents strengthening the preceding result into a ban on all eventual stabilization in an infinite reversible description.

Nor does a deterministic factor of an invertible map always retain its input. On X={0,1}^ℤ shift every coordinate one position, and let E retain the coordinates indexed by n≥0. The resulting F on Y={0,1}^ℕ deletes the first symbol. It is onto and two-to-one. The omitted symbol remains at a negative address in the same whole. This is the standard two-sided extension of the one-sided full shift. [Hochman, Proposition 5.10 and §5.4, Problem 3](https://math.huji.ac.il/~mhochman/courses/TD2022/notes-v3.pdf) treats natural extensions in the compact topological setting. The displayed example needs no probability or topology to verify its maps. In particular, invertibility of U does not give a deterministic factor for U⁻¹ through the same E.

**Preparation has a precise role in an idempotent episode.** Suppose the factor equation is required only on P⊆X. Its exact image equation is

    F(E(P)) = E(U(P)).

If E(U(P))=E(P) and F maps E(P) to itself, the same argument makes any idempotent F the identity on E(P). Therefore a proper idempotent update on that image requires E(U(P)) to be a proper subset of E(P). When F²=F on Y, every configuration whose E-value is outside Fix(F) has all its U-predecessors outside P. Otherwise the factor equation would place that value in F(Y)=Fix(F). Preparation thus excludes specific predecessors of the unsettled answers. This is a restriction on the carried source domain, with no missing distinction sent outside the whole.

Forward invariance of P alone does not restore the prohibition. On the same integer shift, take P={n≥−1}, let B(n)=0 for n<0 and B(n)=1 for n≥0, and put F(0)=F(1)=1. Then U(P)⊊P and B∘U=F∘B throughout P, so one step establishes a permanently retained value on this prepared domain. The equation fails globally: n=−2 and n=−1 have the same current B-answer and different next answers. For a finite forward-invariant P and injective U, by contrast, U(P)=P and the prohibition applies. This is the precise finite-domain limit already used by M33.

The resident law construction in M70 provides the finite static counterpart. The predecessor law on {Never, Lit1} is idempotent, as `LocalResetIsIdempotentOnDeclaredCarrier` now computes. The prepared zero and the recorded image of one have the same current local value zero. Their next whole steps give zero and one respectively. `LocalResetCoarseInputsAgree` and `LocalResetCoarseFutureRefusal` establish these equal inputs and positively different futures. The existing refusal of repeated local reset rejects using the prepared law on the restored phase. The phase and retained source are internal parts of the complete representation.

**Static saturation is a different operation from this global factor.** Intersecting a candidate family with the same established fact twice can be idempotent. Its input is a description together with that fact and their correspondence to the source. This does not assert that the resulting refinement is the exact image of one globally surjective physical step through one unchanged reading. Acquiring a previously unavailable fact can supply another input, change the prepared domain or require a richer reading of the current configuration. VI can express the resulting consequences statically while keeping those source relations explicit. No runtime mechanism follows from this distinction, and the factor obstruction does not prohibit learning inside one whole.

**A new permanent entrance requires arbitrarily deep unsettled predecessors.** Let F:Y→Y be surjective and let R⊆Y satisfy F(R)⊆R. Put A_n=F⁻ⁿ(R). These sets increase with n. If any source enters R from outside it, then R⊊F⁻¹(R). Preimages under a surjection preserve strict inclusion, so A_n⊊A_(n+1) for every n. Equivalently, there are sources whose first entrance takes exactly n+1 steps for every n≥0. The strict difference A_(n+1)\A_n supplies them. This needs neither topology nor a deterministic reading of readiness from its previous value. It concerns the actual state transition and the target region. A uniform entrance bound is already excluded by one proper entrance and global surjectivity.

**Finite certifiability adds a second boundary.** Fix the available source readings. A finite certificate specifies answers at finitely many of their addresses, and its cylinder is all admitted sources with those same answers. Call R finitely certifiable when every y∈R belongs to a cylinder entirely contained in R. The unions of cylinders form the topology of these readings, so this condition says exactly that R is open. It is relative to those readings and to the retained source assumptions. It does not supply an algorithm that finds a certificate or a grammar that validates it.

Suppose this source presentation has the finite-cover property: every cover by such open sets has a finite subcover. Also require that preimages under F of open sets are open. This continuity condition means that a finite certificate about the next source can, at each source where it applies, be supported by finitely many current reading answers. If F is surjective, R is open and forward invariant, and every source reaches R after finitely many steps, then R=Y.

Indeed the increasing open sets A_n cover Y. A finite subcover reduces to A_N=Y for their largest N. Hence F^N(Y)⊆R. Surjectivity gives F^N(Y)=Y, so R=Y. Only the finite-subcover property for this particular countable cover was needed. Compactness is a familiar sufficient condition, stronger than that minimum. No premise E∘F=H∘E for a deterministic readiness update is needed here. Removing forward invariance changes the statement: on a two-state exchange each orbit visits either singleton, although neither singleton records permanent readiness.

VC23 supplies one finite-cover presentation without requiring finitely many sources. Use countably many finite-valued reading addresses and admit exactly the assignments satisfying a stated family of finite-address requirements. Its finite-branching argument gives compactness in the cylinder topology. Equivalently, a full assignment whose every finite restriction extends to an admitted source must itself be admitted. No extra global admission restriction may be discarded. [Hochman, §3.2](https://math.huji.ac.il/~mhochman/courses/TD2022/notes-v3.pdf) describes these cylinder sets for symbolic systems. They are a topology of the chosen presentation, without a metric or topology being assigned to physical reality by this argument.

**One preserved existence fact changes the conclusion.** Let δ_k be the binary pattern on integer addresses with one at k and zeros elsewhere. Take Y={δ_k:k∈ℤ}, with the explicit premise that exactly one marker exists. Let F move the marker from k to k−1, and let R={δ_k:k≤0}. The zero boundary names an internal reading reference in this model. F is bijective, R is proper and forward invariant, and every source reaches R. Reading the one at any k≤0 certifies membership in R. Each δ_k is identified by the single answer at k, using the retained uniqueness premise. Thus R is open and F is continuous.

The finite-cover property fails: the cylinders asserting a one at k cover Y, and no finite subfamily covers it. The all-zero pattern agrees with some member of Y on every finite window but violates the existence premise. Admitting it to complete the pattern space changes the given source family. This is an example about preserving a specified existence fact, not a reduction of encountered qualia or joint contrast to a marker.

Adjoin the all-zero pattern o explicitly and call the enlarged family Ȳ. It is compact: the requirement of at most one marker is the family of finite two-address refusals of simultaneous ones. Extend F by F(o)=o. The same open R now fails universal reachability because o never enters it. If instead readiness is enlarged to R∪{o}, every source reaches readiness, but that readiness is not open. Every finite all-zero window is compatible with a marker farther to the right, outside readiness. Hence o has no finite positive readiness certificate through these coordinate readings. A uniformly sound reader restricted to finitely many such answers cannot certify o: after any finite adaptive transcript of zeros an unqueried positive coordinate still supplies a counter-source. Other access or a retained global premise changes this question.

Projecting Ȳ to its nonnegative coordinates gives the compact one-sided family with at most one marker. Its shift is surjective, and each source eventually becomes the all-zero pattern and remains there. Thus even compactness and continuity do not prohibit pointwise finite stabilization of the whole projected state. Its absorbing zero singleton again lacks a finite certificate. This realizes the earlier unbounded-settling example through binary readings rather than a numerical distance to readiness.

The distinction is therefore between occurrence, permanent target membership, finite evidence for that membership and uniform completion across a source family. A positive global premise can validly remove a limiting counterdescription. VC23 already proves why unbounded existence cannot in general be replaced by only finite-carrier local constraints, even with hidden variables. The source contract must retain that premise or a corresponding witness. Membership of everything in one reality supplies neither the finite-cover property nor a license to insert a description contradicting what was given.

**M35 (The residual required by compression).** Let K be finite, E the visible compressed representation and R an internal residual. Exact recovery of the whole requires `(E,R)` to be injective. For each image e, distinct members of its fiber `E⁻¹(e)` must have different residuals. Therefore a fixed residual alphabet needs at least `max_e |E⁻¹(e)|` values. This bound is attainable by assigning different residual labels inside each fiber. An encoder that uses fewer labels merges some whole states. No external storage is invoked: E and R are parts of the same complete representation.

For random input X and deterministic E(X), R(X) with exact recovery, `H(R|E) = H(X|E)`. Indeed `H(E,R) = H(X)` by injectivity on the input support, and subtracting `H(E)` gives the equality. The residual can require joint reading with E. This statement quantifies where local compression leaves distinctions, without calling those distinctions thermodynamic entropy or requiring an active decoder.

The three binary beam coordinates give eight whole states. The visible two-coordinate image has four fibers of size two, so one residual bit is necessary and sufficient for exact recovery. `BeamWithResidual<B>` retains that coordinate and the Swift checks recover all three original coordinates for arbitrary B. Each of the four fibers must assign its two states different residual labels. Each fiber has two such assignments, independently, giving 2⁴ = 16 recovering binary residual maps. Variable-length coding and physical resource costs require their own representation and cost models.

M70 applies this obligation to an entire class of local laws and their repeated use. A freely supplied internal record permits every local finite deterministic table over any prepared finite horizon. Fixing the carrier and requiring continued autonomous use changes the result: the reset horizon has an exact capacity bound even while the joint entropy remains unchanged.

**M36 (Stationarity prevents strict retaining growth almost surely).** Let U preserve a probability measure μ on the entire Ω. For a measurable real V with V(Ux)≥V(x) μ-almost surely, V(Ux)=V(x) almost surely. Neither invertibility, finite Ω nor integrability of V is needed. Physical closure and entropy preservation do not imply the premise U_*μ=μ.

*Proof.* For every rational r, monotonicity nests {V(Ux)≤r} inside {V(x)≤r}, up to a null set. Measure preservation gives equal probabilities, so their difference has probability zero. Every strict increase crosses a rational r. The countable union of these differences therefore has measure zero. Taking the countable union of its preimages under U gives a single full-measure set where V is constant at every forward step. QED

For a fixed countable fact vocabulary with measurable membership tests, apply this argument to each fact's cache-membership indicator. If facts are never dropped, none are added almost surely under μ. This also covers infinite caches without relying on their cardinality as a scalar progress measure. Fact identities and decoding must remain fixed. Exceptional zero-measure states are not excluded by an almost-sure assertion.

*Quantitative boundary.* For any prepared distribution μ, put μ′=U_*μ and TV(μ,μ′)=sup_A|μ(A)−μ′(A)|. For fixed 0≤V≤1,

    |E_μ[V(Ux)−V(x)]| ≤ TV(μ,μ′).

Indeed E[V]=∫_0^1 P(V>r)dr, and each probability difference is bounded by TV. If V does not decrease on the prepared support, then for ε>0, P_μ(V(Ux)−V(x)≥ε)≤TV(μ,μ′)/ε. Without monotonicity, gains and losses can cancel. Normalizing V matters: this is not a universal bound on learned bits, training loss, physical entropy production or heat.

**M37 (Prepared growth inside one reversible whole).** Let Ω contain a retained L-bit source x and an internal cursor k∈{0,…,L}. The transition U(x,k)=(x,k+1 mod(L+1)) is a permutation. The accessible record C(x,k) is the prefix x_0,…,x_(k−1), with position identities retained by its order. Its accessibility is part of the declared reading model. Decreasing k modulo L+1 is the complete inverse.

Prepare uniform x and k=0. The first L steps reveal one additional source bit each: I(x;C)=0,1,…,L. Whole Shannon entropy remains L bits, but the whole distribution changes because its cursor support moves. At wrap the accessible prefix becomes empty while x survives. Under the stationary uniform distribution over all Ω, growing phases and the reset balance, and mean record length remains L/2. Thus stationarity permits temporary increases when the same process also permits losses.

For uniform x and k uniform on 0,…,L−1, one step increases V=k/L by 1/L on every prepared state. TV(μ,U_*μ)=1/L, attaining M36's bound. Accessible source information increases by one bit and whole entropy is unchanged. The resulting ensemble includes the reset phase, so the same argument cannot be repeated to claim indefinite retention. At L=4 the formula gives TV=1/4.

*Infinite capacity changes the construction.* Take Ω=A^ℕ×ℤ, an immutable infinite source tape and an internal integer cursor. U(x,k)=(x,k+1) is bijective. C(x,k) consists of (i,x_i) for 0≤i<max(k,0), so each record is finite, no fact is lost, and from k=0 each step adds one position. A product tape law with cursor δ_0 evolves to the same tape law with cursor δ_t. No U-invariant probability measure exists on Ω: its integer-cursor marginal would have equal mass at every integer, which cannot sum to one. Product tape measure times counting measure is invariant but has infinite mass. The probability-stationarity premise of M36 therefore fails.

Everything in this construction is inside Ω. It assumes an infinite tape and unbounded cursor, without deriving a finite physical implementation. The cache represents expanding access to an existing internal source. It does not certify an unknown physical law from no evidence.

`ExposeNext` and `RewindExposure` move a resident Pair field into a cache and recover the original fields in pure types. They do not encode a probability measure or establish that an arbitrary Pair is a nonempty tape.
