The stopping outcomes now separate by question. The old two-content output is already computable by the shared decoder, with no law acquisition. The law question with calibrated reserve 2 has a distinguishing available preparation. With calibrated reserve 0 or 1 and only the declared preparations and F/S continuations, the law question remains unidentifiable: the two laws have identical tuple readouts throughout the closed two-content domain. This last conclusion is a certificate about the current interface, not a recovered law answer. Successful reuse requires a recovered answer.

For the explicit six-case domain {F,S}×{0,1,2}, suppose the two allowed new queries are the reserve reading and the first response to preparation (0,1,c), each counted once. Reading the reserve first and reading the response only when c=2 returns either the law or the stated non-identifiability certificate in at most two queries. Two are necessary in the worst case: reserve result 2 still leaves two laws, while response result 1 leaves both an identifiable S/2 case and an unidentifiable F/1 case. The old fact is constant and cannot help. This proves a worst-case count for these queries without a probability prior. It gives no optimal ordering for different costs or expected-cost objectives.

**A finite test of the missing interpretation relation.** Fix positive finite arity n and a total family F_A:Aⁿ→Aⁿ that commutes with every injective map between content sets. A tuple determines a partition π of its n positions: positions share a block exactly when their contents agree. Let B_π be the set of blocks and t_π its canonical tuple, whose i-th entry is the block containing i. The finite collection of outputs r_π=F_Bπ(t_π), one for each partition, determines the family.

Indeed, each tuple x has a unique injective map j:B_π→A assigning each block its actual content. Then x=jⁿ(t_π), and the assumed symmetry gives F_A(x)=jⁿ(r_π). Conversely, any such collection defines an injectively natural family by this equation. Thus relation patterns replace enumeration of arbitrary content values. This conclusion requires compatibility across injective embeddings, including into larger carriers, rather than renaming within one fixed carrier alone.

**Recovery within the equality-sensitive family.** Suppose each F_A is bijective. Its inverse family G also commutes with every injective h: compose F_B ∘ hⁿ = hⁿ ∘ F_A with the two inverses. Both families return only contents present in their input, since each input is the image of its canonical tuple under its support embedding. Applying this observation to F and G shows that F preserves the set of present contents exactly. It need not preserve their multiplicities or which positions agree.

Let Pₙ,ₖ be the partitions of n positions into k blocks, and let S(n,k) be their number. Each r_π must use all k input blocks. Its equality pattern ρ therefore belongs to Pₙ,ₖ, and its entries give a bijection from the output blocks to the input blocks. Invertibility makes π↦ρ a permutation of Pₙ,ₖ: the inverse pattern map recovers π. Conversely, any such permutation, with a block bijection chosen at each π, defines a bijective family through the reconstruction above. Its inverse uses the inverse pattern permutation and inverse block bijections. This classifies all recoverable families under the injective-interpretation premise.

Consequently the number of these families is

```
∏_{k=1}^n S(n,k)! · (k!)^{S(n,k)}.
```

For triples, S(3,1)=1, S(3,2)=3 and S(3,3)=1, giving 1·48·6=288 families. M65's stronger interpretation condition leaves six. The difference is freedom to act differently on equality patterns, not an exterior controller. For example, exchange the repeated and singleton contents on the first=second pattern, (a,a,b)↦(b,b,a), and leave every other pattern unchanged. This is an involution, including the all-equal case where it changes nothing. It exchanges the multiplicities of a and b while preserving the full recoverable input. A frequency of occurrence is therefore not, by itself, the conserved information of this interface.

Within this known reversible family, exact identification by prepared-input queries with complete output readings needs one query in every pattern with at least two blocks. These queries suffice by the table reconstruction. They are necessary because, at any unqueried such pattern, one can change its block bijection alone and retain both reversibility and every other response. The all-equal pattern has only one possible response and carries no law distinction. Thus triples require four queries of distinct equality patterns in this query model, even with adaptive choice. Probes restricted to two available content values can never inspect the all-distinct triple pattern and cannot identify its action. This is a limitation of the preparation interface, not of the number of repeated observations. It also explains why the five-row certificate below checks one automatic equation in the declared family: that row verifies its supplied source and answer, rather than separating candidate laws.

There is an entropy consequence at arity four. Exchange the patterns (a,a,a,b) and (a,a,b,b) for a≠b using the indicated content correspondence, and leave the other patterns unchanged. This is an injectively natural involution. If an internal reading chooses one of the four positions uniformly, its content distribution changes from (3/4,1/4) to (1/2,1/2). Its entropy increases from 2−(3/4)log₂3 to 1 bit. The strict inequality follows from 3³>2⁴. The inverse decreases it again. Meanwhile the entropy of any finite probability ensemble on complete tuples is preserved by the involution, since the probabilities are only reassigned to different complete tuples. Histogram entropy for a position reading and ensemble entropy for the complete source measure different questions. Neither a conserved histogram nor monotonic histogram entropy follows from reversible internal change. No thermodynamic identification or exterior reservoir is used here. `ChangeMultiplicity31To22` and `ChangeMultiplicity22To31` construct the two source-bound clauses from existing projections, and the generic return checks recover both preparations for arbitrary A,B. The zero/successor witness distinguishes their changed third coordinate. The case A=B returns the same tuple and has no histogram contrast.

**A finite return certificate.** Let F and G both commute with every injective interpretation. It suffices to establish G_Bπ(F_Bπ(t_π))=t_π for every equality pattern π. Transport along the support embedding gives G_A F_A=id for every A. For finite A, F_A is an injection of a finite set Aⁿ into itself, hence a bijection with inverse G_A. Thus F_A G_A=id there too. Every tuple over an arbitrary A is supported on a finite subset, so embedding naturality transports this second equality to every A. A finite one-sided return certificate therefore establishes both-sided recovery for these families, without separately requiring the second return.

`EqualityPatternsRecoverTheirPreparedContents` supplies the five return equations for the earlier equality-controlled rule through existing `ReadingFactors`. Its independent question reads the actual prepared contents. The observation applies the rule, and the decoder applies its match clauses to the actual output patterns, which can differ from the input patterns. It derives recovery of all five preparations. A refusal replaces this decoder by identity on the same observed input: source binding succeeds but answer recovery fails. The passage from these finite equations to arbitrary carriers is the proof above under the two embedding premises. The construction does not assert those premises for arbitrary Swift conformances.

**Compatibility with identifications.** Let δ be the discrete partition, with all n positions distinct. The family commutes with every map of content sets exactly when, for every π,

```
r_π = q_πⁿ(r_δ),
```

where q_π sends each distinct generic marker to its π-block. Necessity applies the claimed uniformity to q_π. For sufficiency, r_δ selects an input position for each output. The displayed equations make every r_π use those same positions, with coincident contents identified. The preceding reconstruction then makes F that fixed selection family on every carrier, which commutes with every map. This yields a finite certificate for M65's stronger condition within the stated injective-symmetry family. Recovery then selects the permutations by the earlier proof.

For n=3 there are five patterns: all equal, one of three possible equal pairs, and all distinct. The equality-controlled rule fails the displayed equation on a pair pattern, with the actual witness above. Checking only the all-distinct pattern leaves that dependence unresolved. These are exact equations about a declared law family. A finite set of empirical matches does not establish that an unknown mechanism obeys the symmetry premise. The useful reduction is from arbitrary interpretations to a finite set of relation-preservation obligations once that premise is established.

**The finite pattern certificate in types.** `TripleMarkerProfiles` contains the three input positions, each carrying its five canonical interpretation values. Existing `SelectCandidateLeaf/Left/Right` paths select actual profiles from that same support. `TriplePatternImages` reads their columns and constructs all five predicted output triples. The caller supplies positions, rather than five independently chosen expected answers. Repeated positions are permitted here because the uniformity theorem includes non-injective selections. Recovery is a further obligation.

`TriplePatternCertificate` compares this computed table with an actual `PredicateReading` assembled from five applications by `PairedApplication`. Its source must contain precisely the five prepared inputs, each paired with the same law address. The swap certificate uses existing projection and joint-reading constructors to compute the independent applications. The equality-controlled table has the identity answer on distinct markers but a positively different first coordinate on the first=third pattern. Its certificate fails there. Another refusal changes only the law address of the all-equal row, retaining every answer, and still fails the common-source requirement. A selection from a different support fails too.

The certificate checks these finite source and answer equations. Its selection domain is the existing finite candidate-path grammar, and the global conclusion uses the injective-interpretation premise proved sufficient above. Arbitrary `Pair` conformances are not selection proofs, and an address shared across supplied readings does not establish stationarity of an unknown physical apparatus. Within the declared family, however, the finite table now has a type-checked compatibility certificate instead of five unconnected reported outputs. No runtime comparison or protocol named uniformly proven is introduced.

**The constructed consumer supplies its own uniformity.** Once the five compatibility equations are satisfied, the distinct-marker answer identifies each selected position: the three markers are different. Every other row is its prescribed interpretation image. Thus projection to that one answer is injective on compatible tables and suffices to reconstruct their answers. The five-row obligation establishes compatibility. It need not remain the consumer's memory payload. This is the question-scoped reduction of VC84 applied to this certified family.

`TriplePatternCertificate.Record` composes the actual five-pattern reading with projection to its distinct-marker answer. `AcquireAnswer` records that three-position signature. `TriplePatternCertificate.Use` accepts this fact beside a new arbitrary data triple. Its three data selections must start from that triple's actual candidate support and carry the same path addresses as the certificate's three marker selections. `ReadTripleThroughPaths` returns their selected payloads. Thus the consumer's source includes the required signature fact, and the law positions remain fixed when data values change. `AcquiredPatternLawAppliesToArbitraryData` composes actual acquisition with this consumer and derives the same output as the independent generic swap reading for arbitrary A,B,C. The acquisition's source still binds all five prepared applications. Its fact payload retains one triple.

Uniformity of this generated consumer follows from the path grammar, without inferring it from sampled responses. Interpret payloads by any function h, keeping the candidate-tree branches. At a leaf, selecting after interpretation gives h of the original selected payload. A left or right path preserves its branch choice and applies the child's same relation. Induction proves that selection commutes with h for every finite path. It remains valid when h identifies payload values: their occurrence addresses remain distinct. The address equalities in `Use` bind the marker and data paths, so all three outputs obey M65's stronger interpretation equation.

This supplies the requested structural exclusion of equality-dependent routing for this interface. The admitted consumer chooses by the certified address, and no path constructor changes that address after inspecting a payload. A refusal supplies three equal data values and substitutes the wrong first path: identical current answers do not satisfy the unequal address types. Another supplies a different signature with the same context, question and triple shape. It fails the required fact input. A compact signature suffices after certification, whereas observing that signature alone does not establish compatibility of an unknown law. The construction establishes uniform use of certified positions. Applying it to that other mechanism requires a binding to this grammar or the stated symmetry family.

## §4h. Recovering dependence from admissible records

**M66 (when records determine a fixed prerequisite order).** Fix a finite set E of addressed occurrences inside the whole and a family C of admitted partial records, each represented by its subset of E. Require ∅ and E to belong to C. Membership means that an occurrence is included in that record, not that every internal reader can recover it. The family specifies possible records for this inquiry. It is not a population of realities outside Ω, and E need not exhaust the whole.

Define a relation without a prior clock:

    e ≼ f  iff  every H in C containing f also contains e.

This relation is reflexive and transitive. Every admitted record is downward closed for it: recording f requires recording each e≼f. The converse is exact under the following condition:

    C is all downward-closed subsets for ≼
        iff C is closed under unions and intersections.

*Proof.* Downward-closed subsets are closed under both operations. Conversely, suppose C has those closures. For f∈E, let P_f be the intersection of all admitted records containing f. There is at least one such record, E. The intersection is admitted and contains exactly the e with e≼f. Every downward-closed D is the union of P_f over f∈D. Each P_f is contained in D, and each f belongs to its P_f, proving equality. Union closure and ∅∈C now give D∈C. Finiteness makes the displayed unions and intersections finite. QED

The relation is antisymmetric exactly when C separates each pair of distinct occurrences: some admitted record contains one without the other. If two addresses always occur together, their mutual prerequisite relation is a preorder equivalence. Quotienting by that equivalence gives a partial order on the distinguishable blocks. It does not prove that the original occurrences were identical. Their separation may require a richer record interface.

These are constraints on record inclusion. Interpreting them as causal requirements of a process additionally needs the process's actual admissible transitions. A selected sample, a reporting convention or a restricted preparation can correlate entries without making one event produce another. Internal closure by itself establishes none of those transition bindings.

Under both closures and pair separation, this is the unique partial order whose downward-closed records are C. Indeed, every partial order can be recovered from its downward-closed sets by the displayed test: if e is not below f, the principal past P_f contains f and excludes e. This proves uniqueness from the admitted family, rather than choosing an order and calling it necessary. A sampled subfamily can introduce false prerequisites by missing its distinguishing records. Coverage of C remains an obligation.

Union and intersection obey the distributive set laws here, but complementation need not give an admitted record. For a strict prerequisite a≺b, the complement of P_a contains b and omits a, so it is not downward closed. Under the closure and separation premises, every complement is admitted exactly when the order has no strict prerequisites. In that case every subset is admitted. Thus this algebra of histories is generally not Boolean. This does not reject classical reasoning about sets. It prevents interpreting independent Boolean choices as the admissible event combinations when actual prerequisites exclude some of them.

The closure conditions have content. Take E={a,b,c} and admit every subset except {c}. Here c requires at least one of a or b. Both {a,c} and {b,c} are admitted, but their intersection {c} is not. No distinct individual occurrence is required by c in every admitted record. The recovered preorder is therefore equality, whose downward-closed family would incorrectly admit {c}. A single fixed prerequisite order loses the alternative support. A second obstruction is incompatibility: for E={a,b,c}, the family ∅,{a},{b},E has both endpoints but excludes {a,b}. Its fixed prerequisite relation also admits {a,b}. The failure of union closure carries information that pairwise precedence alone misses.

The first family has a static realization. Interpret Never and Lit1 as the absence and presence of each of two addressed prerequisite records. `WitnessedOccurrence<R>` appends a successful actual reading, retaining its input and the operation that supplied its Lit1 answer. Admit the two templates FirstOccurrence<B> and SecondOccurrence<A> in Checks/ClosedInformation.swift, with A and B restricted to Never or Lit1. These fix both the actual projection and its recorded operation name. A first projection accepts exactly the inputs with a present. A second projection accepts exactly those with b present. Neither accepts (Never,Never). Together with the four inputs before appending c, their projected occurrence sets are precisely the seven members of C. This exhaustiveness refers to that finite projection grammar, not to arbitrary conformances of PredicateReading.

`OccurrenceImage<R>` reads the prior pair and the appended answer from the actual record, omitting the operation field. On the input (Lit1,Lit1), the two successful projections give the same occurrence image while the complete records retain different operations. Generic checks derive both construction families, source recovery and operation retention. Negative checks reject appending with either absent witness, identifying the complete records from their common image, and reading a different construction's source. The disjunction has not lost its realized support from the whole. This particular interface has omitted which support supplied it.

Thus absence of an outside does not force every internal description to have a fixed prerequisite order. A description can merge different internal supports. Recovering a richer relation requires retaining or reading those supports. M66 gives the precise closure criterion for when the simpler description suffices. The proof about arbitrary finite families is the set argument above. The Swift construction realizes its alternative-support obstruction and retained resolution, without claiming to encode every finite order.

The transfer question already has a kernel in VC60–VC62. A common decoded answer does not require common histories, and an established agreement on the actual resident source can reuse an observation through AnswerThroughAgreement. VC83 handles a changed mechanism for a specified question. VC84 separates the minimum answer content from the addressed inputs that can provide it: several incomparable input subsets may each suffice. Their intersection need not answer the question. Alternative realized support therefore needs neither a new equality principle nor a unique chosen carrier.

**M67 (a fixed order determines its permitted serializations, not a universal clock).** Apply M66 when its closure and separation premises are satisfied. A maximal chain of admitted records from ∅ to E adds exactly one occurrence at each step, and its sequence respects ≼. Conversely, any ordering of E respecting ≼ has admitted prefixes and gives such a chain. For the first direction, if a chain step added more than one occurrence, choose a minimal new occurrence. Adding it alone to the earlier record gives an intermediate downward-closed record, contradicting maximality. The converse follows because each prefix contains every prerequisite of its members.

For distinct e,f, e≼f exactly when every such serialization puts e before f. One direction follows from the prefix requirement. If e is not below f, add f before e to the finite order. This creates no cycle, since a cycle would require an existing path from e to f. Repeatedly selecting a minimal remaining element gives a serialization of the augmented order, and hence one of the original order with f before e. QED

There is exactly one serialization iff the order is total. An incomparable pair permits both relative orders by the same argument. Thus a chain recording a before b does not establish that their common prerequisites force that order. A particular internal recorder can add and retain that distinction. Its chosen chain then belongs to the richer record being described.

Any two serializations can be connected by exchanges of adjacent incomparable occurrences. To prove this, take the first occurrence of the target serialization and move it leftward in the other. Each crossed occurrence is incomparable with it: a required predecessor would also have preceded it in the target, and a required successor could not precede it in the current valid sequence. Fix that first position and repeat on the remaining finite sequence. Consequently, a question about serializations factors through the underlying partial order exactly when its answer is unchanged by every such exchange. This is the existing question-factorization criterion applied to the connected family of serializations. The question may still distinguish other partial orders.

G14b constructs the local answer agreement for actual disjoint pair applications. Extending that agreement through a larger execution additionally requires valid intermediate applications and preservation by their surrounding operations. M67 does not infer commutation of arbitrary effects from a diagram. It also does not erase a recorder's selected order from the full retained history.

Neither the number of occurrences nor the number of serializations supplies physical duration or an entropy law. An entropy over serializations first requires that those alternatives be distinguished by the declared reading and assigned probabilities. An append-only record can have a strict prefix order without increasing uncertainty, as M48 and M63 already distinguish. The fundamental gain is a reconstruction question with an exact answer: which prerequisite relations does this admitted record family force, and what information does a simpler order omit?

Space S27 now identifies one missing relation precisely. Even a linearly ordered real carrier with a retained unit interval admits different positive additive durations. A supplied hierarchy of equal halves, in contrast, forces the normalized interval answer to agree in all increasing real readings. Such a reading exists exactly when its grid separates every distinct ordered pair of occurrences. The proof does not require the occurrence carrier to fill a continuum. Finite depth gives a bound unless an exact constructed endpoint is identified. The value carrier matters: lexicographically ordered pairs retain all midpoint relations while permitting a change below every real subdivision of one reference unit. Thus an internal clock reconstruction needs justified interval comparisons and a value contract, beyond the prerequisite order reconstructed here.

**Two-sided extension gives an order of regions without choosing an event orientation.** Fix an internal occurrence g and two addressed arms of occurrences extending from it. Let I[m,n] retain g, the first m occurrences of the first arm and the first n of the second, with the same occurrence correspondences and all specified relations on those retained occurrences. Assume all the considered pairs of prefixes are jointly admitted. Then actual inclusion gives

    I[m,n] ⊆ I[p,q]  iff  m≤p and n≤q.

Either arm can extend while the other is retained. Adding one occurrence at each arm gives the same region in either order. The two intermediate regions differ. If the arms have interchangeable content and contact laws, their exchange preserves this inclusion relation and fixes the central region. It does not select a first arm. A chain of construction steps records an additional choice of order, as M67 already proves. The arms and their joint availability are premises, rather than consequences of a centre having two incidences.

This permits a precise meaning of construction in both directions: the retained region can extend at either end. It does not yet make one end past and the other future. A record-dependency law in which each new occurrence retains its inward predecessor points outward on both arms. A line orientation through g points inward on one arm and outward on the other. Reading the common incidence pattern does not equate those laws. Nor does revealing an earlier part of a description show that the earlier event was produced by that revelation.

The static example now includes identical local rules at different places. `RecordedIndependentOrders<F,G>` lifts each rule to the full paired source, composes the two valid orders and retains their actual lifted applications. Its generic equality recovers the same initial source and final answer. `SameRuleAtTwoSides` uses one root-growth rule on both fields. The final regions coincide, but the first recorded applications have answers `(node,empty)` and `(empty,node)`. `DISTANCE_SAME_LOCAL_RULE_ERASES_ORDER` rejects identity of those complete records. Keeping only the local rule name would record the same rule twice in either order and lose which place changed first. These are represented constructions, not assertions that every formal serialization is a physically admitted history.

**A scalar clock can increase without representing the complete order.** On the prefix regions, τ(m,n)=m+n strictly increases on every proper inclusion. Yet I[1,0] and I[0,1] are distinct and receive the same value. This equality alone is no contradiction: distinct incomparable occurrences may share a date. Indeed the four-region square with m,n∈{0,1} admits a scalar representation of its strict order by that rank, with the two middle regions tied. It fails to identify the regions, but it preserves that strict-order question.

The next finite pattern excludes even this weaker scalar representation. Set A=I[1,0], B=I[0,1] and C=I[2,0]. Then A is a proper part of C, while B is incomparable with each. If a reading into one linear order satisfied

    H is a proper part of K  iff  τ(H)<τ(K),

incomparability would force τ(A)=τ(B)=τ(C), contradicting τ(A)<τ(C). Thus no scalar reading can preserve and reflect all those precedence answers, even with tied dates. The witness has only three regions and remains an obstruction in any extension preserving those relations. Choosing a scalar required only to increase on the admitted inclusions is still possible. Its other numerical comparisons cannot all be read back as prerequisites. The three records already have finite tree presentations in G1's actual address grammar: a common root with only its left child occupied, only its right child occupied, or its left child and that child's children occupied.

**Independent records force a lower bound on exact ordered coordinates.** Let n≥1 addressed occurrences be independently retainable over one common record: every subset gives an admitted record, with its original occurrence identities. Suppose a k-coordinate reading T into a product of linear orders is required to preserve and reflect inclusion by componentwise comparison:

    H⊆K  iff  T_j(H)≤T_j(K) for every j=1,…,k.

Then k≥n, and n presence coordinates attain the bound. To prove the lower bound, omit the common record from notation and put A_i={i}, B_i={1,…,n}∖{i}. Since A_i is not contained in B_i, some coordinate must have T_j(A_i)>T_j(B_i). A single coordinate cannot serve two different i and l: the crossed inclusions A_i⊆B_l and A_l⊆B_i would give

    T_j(A_i) ≤ T_j(B_l) < T_j(A_l) ≤ T_j(B_i) < T_j(A_i).

Hence the n refusals need n different coordinates. The upper bound reads whether each actual occurrence is present. QED

This is the standard order-dimension obstruction, whose singleton/complement example is recalled by [Kim et al., §4](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1803.08641). Its use here is a finite refusal of an underspecified clock representation. The lower bound needs only those witnessed records, their crossed inclusions and their omitted occurrences, not a catalogue of the entire whole. It constrains an exact product-order reading. A single real number can encode a finite record with an arbitrary decoder, but its ordinary numerical order then does not supply this inclusion relation. Other relations may accompany a scalar physical clock and carry what its date omits. No claim of n spatial dimensions or n fundamental physical times follows from the bound.

**Past, future and traversal have different addresses.** For a specified strict event order ≺ and an occurrence e, define P(e)={x:x≺e} and F(e)={x:e≺x}. One relation x≺e gives x its predecessor role relative to e and e its successor role relative to x. The two roles are determined together. In an admitted record extension adding y and the relation x≺y, the retained occurrence x also acquires its past role relative to y. The extension adds a relation involving x without replacing its earlier recorded content. A reading of that old content need not describe x's enlarged relational context. An occurrence incomparable with e belongs to neither P(e) nor F(e). Thus the complement of a chosen past need not be its future. These definitions use the declared order. They cannot orient a general contact or turn mutual support into temporal precedence.

For a≺c and b≺c with a and b incomparable, both a,b,c and b,a,c serialize the same prerequisite structure. Their first item differs, so firstness in that list is insufficient to identify an intrinsic first event. If an internal recorder distinguishes which it acquired first, its acquisition adds an ordered relation to the full account. Forgetting that recorder can recover the common prerequisite image. Keeping it can distinguish the two acquisitions. The reader and its traversal remain inside reality in both descriptions. A change of enumeration is harmless to a question exactly under M67's exchange criterion, rather than because all traversals are physically interchangeable.

M72 expresses the same distinction at a contact boundary. A reading on either side can restrict which descriptions of the other side fit the same actual joining. Learning a later record can therefore determine an earlier answer that was previously open in the account. This statement does not supply an operation rewriting the occurrence. Likewise, listing every member of a finite event family is different from possessing a readable representation of all reality. A relational description can specify a question while leaving most of the whole undescribed.

Neither a finished graph viewed from outside nor a graph growing against an external clock is required by these arguments. In the record model, proper extension is an internal relation between records. In an account of actual becoming, what extends, which occurrence establishes that extension and how it can be registered must belong to the account. Calling the whole static or growing without a distinguishing internal relation does not settle that question. A global numerical time coordinate is a further presentation to justify. Even when an order admits such a coordinate, its numerical origin, scale and a unique traversal need not be determined by that order.

**A reversible capacity constraint can escape every shorter compatibility check.** M67's record-family premises cannot be recovered from pairwise compatibility alone. For any m≥3, take the forms K={0,1}ᵐ with the all-one form omitted. A coordinate toggle is admitted exactly when its result also belongs to K. Every admitted toggle has the same toggle as its inverse. From 0ᵐ, any sequence filling at most m−1 distinct coordinates is admitted in every order, and the result depends only on which coordinates were filled. Filling all m is forbidden in every order. Thus checks of every shorter distinct-filling sequence miss the joint obstruction. No fixed test depth covers all such media as m grows.

This is also a limit on interpreting resistance as information loss. Each admitted transition is one-to-one and reversible, yet the medium excludes a continuation. The unrestricted bit-flip formulas commute, but they forget the missing form. The partial laws agree on final values wherever both orders are admitted. Their domains can nevertheless differ: start with only j vacant, clear an occupied i, then fill j. Toggling j first would leave no vacancy and is forbidden. The toggle values alone therefore omit part of the interaction law. VC46–VC47 already specify the missing domain relation.

For m=3, the admitted forms after filling each pair retain a directly checked vacancy. All three possible vacancy clauses reject 111 in `Checks/Interactions.swift`. These are static compatibility witnesses, not a physical filling process or a universal transition implementation. The model's capacity law is stipulated. It is a counterconstruction to the proposed inference from reversible local behavior to unrestricted joint availability.

VC23b then asks what internal form can witness that capacity condition. For three positions, three central states express its existence through binary contacts. A deterministic witness acquisition respecting all position permutations needs seven states. A cyclic orientation reduces that conditional bound to four, and a total order permits three. The change concerns actual relations of the chosen medium. No observer is added as a primitive to select a vacant position. The bound identifies what such a selection would require. A physical interpretation would still have to establish the realized contact relations, available acquisitions and resource measure.

Liquid LQ12–LQ14 lift this question to the state of the medium itself. LQ13 derives which replacements preserve an internal contact: a retained partner permits a uniform local replacement exactly when its compatibility set is included in the replacement's set. Equality gives the reversible case. The least complete boundary is determined by these sets, and need not be one identical value on both sides. LQ14 derives a necessary coordination scale by considering all admitted paths, including internal context changes. Vacancy, parity and repetition constraints require widths one, two and n in their complete compatibility envelopes. A temporary internal release of an equality contact can reduce a width-two replacement to width-one steps before restoring the contact. This gives a precise resistance question before assigning energy, mass or duration: which bounded changes cannot fit the actual relations, even through their permitted detours?

LQ15–LQ16 then ask which decomposition those questions may use. Independent blocks are recovered from free splices under the stated finite or finite-character conditions. A partial contact can instead determine two coupled roles through exact self-contact and contextual substitution. Retaining both complementary recombinations gives an involution wherever both are admitted. In the vacancy relation, one result can fit while its complement cannot, so two individually admitted sources do not guarantee this closed exchange. A proposed physical account of resistance must establish the full internal completion, including its admission, rather than assume that a legal local result has somewhere compatible to place every omitted distinction.

## §4i. What an unrestricted internal remainder permits

The gap after M29 is larger than one closed reset counterexample. Suppose the stronger recovery requirement is granted. Does that now select which local changes the whole can realize? M35 bounds a remainder once its carrier is fixed. The following construction tests what remains of that restriction when the carrier may still be supplied freely. All preparation, records and control positions below belong to the same declared whole.

W below describes complete configurations of the declared model, including every resident degree of freedom used by U. Finiteness of W is a premise for the recurrence and capacity results. It is not derived from finiteness of individual descriptions, and it does not identify a current configuration with Ω's entire history. Requiring all occurrence distinctions to remain in a present recoverable record is a stronger condition, addressed separately. No past occurrence is made external to reality by distinguishing its historical relation from a current record of it.

**M70 (unrestricted internal completion does not select a local deterministic law).** Let S be a nonempty finite local carrier and f:S→S any function. There is a finite complete carrier W, a permutation U of W and an internal prepared record b such that the local output of U(s,b) is f(s) for every s. The construction can respect every symmetry of S that respects f. More strongly, for any finite horizon N there is such a whole whose local outputs are fᵗ(s) for every 0≤t≤N. Thus closure, complete recovery and these symmetries do not by themselves rule out any finite local function over a prepared finite horizon. They constrain its completion and reuse when the actual internal resources are specified.

The differences counted in S are represented source distinctions. Unregistered names do not supply additional physical states. Any fixed internal reference or structure grounding those distinctions belongs to the complete model too. The theorem does not derive S, its interpretation or a supply of physical record forms from nonemptiness.

**One use with a complete inverse.** Let E be the disjoint union of one ready form b and one record [s] for each s∈S. Put W=S×E. For each source s, exchange the two whole forms

    p_s = (s,b),             q_s = (f(s),[s]).

Every p_s is different from every q_r because ready and recorded forms are disjoint. Distinct q_s retain distinct s even when their local outputs agree. These exchanges are therefore disjoint pairs. Define U by those exchanges and fix every other whole form. Then U² is the identity on all W, and the prepared use has exactly the requested local output. This is a permutation of a fixed carrier; the record was available before use. Applying U again restores both the source and readiness. It also undoes the local result, so this inverse is not a fresh use of f on that result.

If a permutation g of S satisfies gf=fg, let it fix b and carry [s] to [g(s)]. It sends p_s to p_g(s) and q_s to q_g(s), so it commutes with U. The record inherits the source's relational transformation; no choice of unrelated labels or preferred source is needed. This does not contradict LQ16's symmetry obstruction for a prescribed residual carrier. It supplies a different carrier whose source records have the required symmetry.

The local law can also occupy an internal address. For any finite family of functions, take the disjoint sectors (f,s,e), leave the f address unchanged and use the corresponding exchange in each sector. One complete permutation covers all the sectors. In particular, F=S^S is finite, so one fixed whole W=F×S×E admits every local table as a prepared internal mode. This strengthens the quantifiers: a separate complete dynamical rule need not be chosen for each local f. Different mode values are differences within the complete input, not competing results of an identical input. A written label alone would not establish the evaluation relation; the construction uses each actual function table. It does not establish that a particular physical medium contains those tables or those record forms.

For F=S^S, any relabeling g of S acts on the entire preparation by (f,s,e)↦(gfg⁻¹,g(s),g_E(e)). The complete U respects this action too: the relabeled table applied to g(s) gives g(f(s)), and the record becomes [g(s)]. Thus allowing the law form itself to transform does not break the construction. Holding one f fixed recovers the earlier symmetry statement. This checks the local rule and its record together, rather than placing a fixed rule outside the whole and renaming only its input.

**Any finite number of successive uses.** For N≥1, choose E={b} ⊔ (S×{1,…,N}). For each initial s, make one cycle

    (s,b) → (f(s),(s,1)) → … → (fᴺ(s),(s,N)) → (s,b).

All cycles are disjoint: the recorded source and internal position distinguish their members. Fix every unused member of S×E. This again gives a permutation of a finite fixed whole and commutes with every symmetry of f. The position belongs to E; counting applications does not supply a controller outside the whole. The first N outputs realize the successive local law. Given the retained source, position and law, one uniform reading also recovers the finite occurrence record (s,f(s),…,fᵗ(s)) at every prepared position t≤N. Those records extend properly even when successive local values agree. The cycle's last return retracts that record, so the construction does not satisfy indefinite strict retention. For N=0, the identity suffices.

**Unlimited autonomous reuse changes the answer.** On a finite whole, a permutation eventually returns each complete prepared source to itself. If one fixed U were to display fᵗ(s) for every t≥0 and every prepared s, some p>0 would therefore satisfy fᵖ(s)=s for each s. Every member of the finite S would be periodic, which forces f to be a bijection. Conversely, a bijective f acts directly as a permutation on S. Hence indefinite exact repetition from every local source in a finite reversible whole is possible exactly for bijective f. With a restricted preparation, the necessary condition concerns its prepared sources: each must lie on a cycle of f. An internal controller, clock or spare record belongs to the finite whole in this argument. Repeated external resets of such a controller would change the stated boundary. M51 supplies the recurrence principle; here it restricts an entire proposed local law. The quantifiers distinguish the results: a suitable whole for each finite horizon does not give one finite whole suitable for every horizon.

**Indefinite proper records still do not select f if their carrier is unrestricted.** Admit the finite histories h_t(s)=(s,f(s),…,fᵗ(s)) for every t≥0. Appending f of the last entry defines one total internal extension on this countably infinite history carrier. Removing the final entry recovers its source on the extension's range. Every record retains the initial source and every earlier position, and each extension is proper, including when f repeats a value. The local reading at the last entry obeys the same arbitrary f. The law can again occupy an unchanged internal address. This construction satisfies faithful retention and room for another finite record; it assumes an unbounded admitted record carrier. It is injective and invertible on its image, not a permutation of a finite whole. It therefore cannot be used to evade the finite-carrier theorem above. The record requirement constrains the complete histories without selecting a particular local f when this further resource remains free.

**An exact boundary without first selecting a memory.** Let L:W→S name the local form, with |S|=n and a distinguished 0∈S. For each s, let p_s be an admitted prepared complete configuration with L(p_s)=s. Suppose one permutation U keeps the local result at 0 for the first N≥1 applications after every p_s. The rest of each preparation need not be identical, and W need not be a product of independently supplied parts. Write W₀={w∈W:L(w)=0}. Then

    |W₀| ≥ 1 + (n−1)N.

To prove this, consider Uᵗ(p_s) for s≠0 and 1≤t≤N. All have local value 0. They are pairwise distinct. An equality at equal t would identify initial sources by invertibility. If t>u, an equality Uᵗ(p_s)=Uᵘ(p_r) would give Uᵗ⁻ᵘ(p_s)=p_r: the left has local value 0 and the right r≠0. Thus these forms use (n−1)N distinct zero-local states. The further state Uᴺ(p_0) is outside this family. Otherwise Uᴺ(p_0)=Uᵘ(p_r) would give Uᴺ⁻ᵘ(p_0)=p_r, again equating a zero local value with r≠0. This yields the additional state and the bound. Only the actual local distinctions, preparations and one-to-one complete transition were used. L is the specified local distinction in this inquiry, not an observer placed outside reality.

If a remainder reading R:W→E additionally gives a faithful complete description (L,R), it must distinguish all members of W₀. Its carrier E therefore needs at least the same number of forms. This derives the residual capacity obligation after establishing the necessary multiplicity of the whole. Changing which fields are called memory, law or control does not remove it. The bound also holds for an injective U that is not onto: cancelling equal finite iterates in the proof uses injectivity alone.

The same cancellation has a consequence for one prepared trajectory, without a family of alternative initial sources. Suppose L(p)≠0 but L(Uᵗ(p))=0 for every t≥1, with U injective. No two Uᵗ(p) can agree: cancelling a repeated position would return p after a positive number of steps, contradicting its different local value. An indefinitely settled local change therefore requires endlessly distinct complete configurations along that continuation. This is the nonrecurrence consequence of M51, not a derivation of a universal clock or of permanent settling from a finite observation. It also does not construct an internal reader of the position. It identifies a way that continued local stability can require changing distinctions beyond that local reading even without postulating an appended readable history.

The bound is attained. Take E={b} ⊔ ((S∖{0})×{1,…,N}). Fix p_0. For each nonzero s, cycle p_s through (0,(s,1)), …, (0,(s,N)) and back to p_s. Fix everything else. This is autonomous, finite and reversible; it realizes the reset for the requested horizon and respects every permutation of the nonzero source forms. The local value returns to s at the next application. The proof is uniform in n and N, not a count inferred from a finite program run.

If a single internal reading must additionally recover the initial source and distinguish every recorded occurrence position 0≤t≤N, the exact bound strengthens to

    |W₀| ≥ 1 + nN,           hence |E| ≥ 1 + nN for a faithful (L,R).

Now all Uᵗ(p_s), with s∈S and 1≤t≤N, must differ, even at unequal positions, because their recoverable records differ. They must also differ from p_0, whose occurrence record has length zero. All these nN+1 forms have local value 0. The finite-horizon construction above attains the bound with E={b} ⊔ (S×{1,…,N}). Thus the extra demand of recording each occurrence has a concrete consequence even when the local source already equals zero. This demand is stronger than reversibility or the continued equality of local values. The counting argument for these faithful records does not itself need invertibility; the record already separates the source-position pairs.

**Entropy does not replace the resource boundary.** Under a uniform preparation of the n initial local values and one ready remainder, the joint Shannon entropy is log₂n throughout either permutation construction. After a reset, the local entropy is zero and the remainder's entropy at each fixed position is log₂n. These values do not grow with N, while the required remainder carrier does. For n=1, a strict record of N occurrences still needs N+1 remainder forms although the ensemble entropy is zero at every position. The extra forms distinguish positions of a possible retained history; they are not extra uncertainty about the initial source. Uncertainty about the position itself would require another specified probability law. Equal entropy is also not stationarity of the prepared distribution, as M36 requires.

`ReadyLawState<S>` and `RecordedLawState<S>` express the two exchanged phases in bare Swift. The local successor is S.Right in the supplied law graph. The recorded phase carries both that actual successor and S; its next phase is the original preparation. Their generic double-step equalities establish the involution on this declared two-phase family. `ReadPreparedLawSource<O>` reconstructs the prepared fields from the output record alone, without an earlier-source parameter. The generic source-separation proof derives equal sources from equal complete output fields. The existing Never/Succ predecessor relation supplies a reset on its zero/one subfamily: local outputs agree, while the records differ and the second complete step restores the original one. Expected refusals exclude source recovery from just the local equality, indefinite reset by repeating this exchange, identification of ready and recorded zero, and substitution of a different local result. These are the constructive phase and field relations. The general carrier, symmetry and horizon bounds are proved above; open Pair conformance does not seal a finite law graph or prove those cardinalities.

The theorem does not prescribe physical time, heat, energy or which permutations an actual medium permits. Its role in the foundation is exact: allowing an unspecified internal remainder makes closure and recovery compatible with every finite local deterministic table for every separately prepared finite horizon. A restriction on local dynamics must therefore use an independently justified constraint on the remainder, its preparation, its contacts, its available transitions or its continued reuse. The Cut's assertion that no distinction leaves the whole cannot supply those resource relations by renaming the omitted information. Conversely, the tight bounds show where those relations begin to exclude alternatives without changing the no-outside premise.

Preserving source identity is also weaker than respecting every specified relation on prepared sources and their joint extensions. IiS14 excludes transposition in its fixed quantum model because a particular joint input would receive a negative weight. M70 does not supply a completion in that same model: its freely available source records and permutations have not been shown to preserve the quantum preparation and overlap relations. The obstruction concerns the actual joint medium. Calling a source recoverable or assigning it another label does not discharge that obligation.

IiS20–IiS21 now give the complete pure-output test within that model: the source Gram matrix must be the entrywise product of the requested local Gram matrix and one positive unit-diagonal residual matrix. A cyclic family of any size can fail this test at every residual dimension while each proper preparation subfamily admits a unitary transformation. The constructed families also have equal pairwise overlap probabilities and equal mixture entropies for every matching probability law. Their differing cycle relation excludes the common transition. This supplies a restriction that neither identity retention, additional blank capacity nor even that entire entropy function can replace.

The same construction has a precise internal escape. An already prepared two-dimensional record, related differently to the source preparations, admits the transition; a one-dimensional record cannot. The additional record cannot be appended undisturbingly from the original connected family alone. Thus the whole need not acquire an outside exception. What changes is the specified input relation and its availability to the operation. Arbitrary room after a change and an actual correlated preparation before it are different resources. The result selects allowable transitions under those established relations, while the origin of that inner-product and tensor medium remains a separate question.

The unbounded retaining case has no second implementation in code: `ExtendLawRecord<X>` aliases the existing `RetainedWhole<X,X.Right.Right>`. X contains its previous record and its current local law form; the appended local form follows that source's actual Right relation. Generic proofs derive one and two successor steps and recovery of the complete previous record. Two zero successors agree locally while their records remain distinct. This is the admitted finite record grammar, with no bound asserted on all its members. It realizes the difference between returning to preparation and retaining another occurrence through the same underlying local law.

**Contact calibration can constrain even an unrestricted remainder.** Verdict
VC104–VC105 supply a complementary case to M70. Four actual contacts project
to the exchanges between a center and four leaves. Complete endpoint
calibration means that equality of their full final stage permutations also
gives equality of their complete actions. An autonomous selected step must
be one of those actual contacts, including its relevant internal update.
Under these relations, an uninterrupted residence of m leaf readings followed
by exit has exact minimum carrier sizes 5,10,15,30,40,60,120 over the ranges
specified in VC105. Twenty-five equal leaf readings followed by exit are
incompatible with complete calibration at every carrier size. Infinite
unreachable copies cannot change the finite orbit of the given preparation.

The distinction used here is supplied by later behavior, without assuming a
permanently growing record. If two positions before the observed exit were
the same complete source, the same autonomous continuation would make the
exit occur earlier. The remaining number of actual selected contacts to
exit therefore distinguishes those positions. It does not assign a duration
in seconds or require a clock outside the whole. The minimum constructions
even admit reversible U: the selected path closes while other continuations
retain the distinctions needed for the residence question.

This constrains the internal interaction relations, beyond the amount
of blank memory. At four leaf readings, twenty states suffice by a raw count
but their completely calibrated contact geometry is a star with no required
four-vertex path; thirty are necessary and sufficient. At twenty-five leaf
readings, some stage-returning word must have a further distinguishable
action. VC104a bounds such a word by twenty-four contacts and its subsequent
U separator by nineteen. Actual contact addresses and the route to a new
comparison remain acquisition questions. In M70 the unspecified remainder
was allowed to supply arbitrary complete transitions; imposing these common
action relations removes that freedom and yields a definite obstruction.

## §5. Candidate Physical Correspondences

These are questions for an implementation, not completed VI instances. A physical correspondence must declare its state carrier, reading, dynamical operation, record and cost before the relevant conditional theorem applies. Finite dimension does not imply finitely many states. A stable-looking record does not establish indefinite faithful retention. The earlier tables marked several such missing premises as verified; those verdicts are withdrawn.

### Quantum measurement

**Projective branch calculation.** Fix a nonzero orthogonal projector P on a finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space. In the ideal Lüders instrument, an input density operator ρ with p=Tr(Pρ)>0 gives the conditional output L_P(ρ)=PρP/p. The instrument and positive-probability branch are premises. The [measurement formulation](https://quantum.cloud.ibm.com/learning/en/courses/general-formulation-of-quantum-information/general-measurements/formulations-of-measurements) separates the outcome law from the post-measurement state. [Shor's lecture](https://math.mit.edu/~shor/18.435/oldlectures/lecture6.html) gives the projective update and explains why outcome probabilities alone do not specify a general instrument's residual state.

The map L_P is constant on all such inputs exactly when rank(P)=1. If P=|u⟩⟨u| with u normalized, then PρP=⟨u|ρ|u⟩P=pP, so L_P(ρ)=P. Conversely, if rank(P)>1, choose orthogonal unit vectors u and v in its range. Their density operators each have p=1 and are unchanged by L_P, so the two outputs differ. This proves necessity and sufficiency for the stated instrument and unrestricted input domain. Restricting prior inputs can change that conclusion.

Thus a nondegenerate outcome fixes one post-measurement ray, or its density operator, rather than one vector including an arbitrary global phase. For rank g>1, there are continuously many rays within the eigenspace, not g possible pure states. The integer g counts independent basis directions. Even rank one need not identify the input: whenever the whole Hilbert space has another direction v orthogonal to u, the states u and (u+v)/√2 both permit that outcome and give the same conditional output. A single certain outcome likewise need not identify a state: P=I on a space of dimension greater than one leaves every input unchanged.

Quantum.swift checks a supplied recursive dimension expression against One. HydrogenLevel2 supplies four basis-direction slots, without deriving a Hamiltonian or enumerating all rays in its eigenspace. QuantumMeasurement.swift's chosen SolutionSet=Never supplies no projector, update or detector. These declarations therefore cannot replace the calculation above. An actual VI correspondence must bind its target to the post-measurement ray and its instrument to the stated update. The conditioned subsystem update does not establish global information loss or irreversibility. A detector and retained accessible record require the separate relations of M32 and V=I GC3 within the same whole.

**Response equality and certain discrimination.** A qubit supplies three different identification questions. With Pauli matrices X,Y,Z, write a density operator as ρ=(I+rₓX+rᵧY+r_zZ)/2, where r is real and |r|≤1. A measurement along a unit vector n has effects (I±n·σ)/2 and probabilities (1±n·r)/2, by taking the trace. Exact knowledge of the three response distributions along X,Y,Z determines r and hence ρ. This is injectivity of a map to probability laws. Finite experiments estimate those laws from repeated preparations, rather than returning their exact real parameters from one specimen. [IBM's discrimination and tomography formulation](https://quantum.cloud.ibm.com/learning/en/courses/general-formulation-of-quantum-information/general-measurements/discrimination-and-tomography) states the different access assumptions.

For example, |+⟩=(|0⟩+|1⟩)/√2 and |−⟩=(|0⟩−|1⟩)/√2 have the same Z outcome law, (1/2,1/2), but opposite certain X outcomes. A different measurement separates this pair. The states |0⟩ and |+⟩ already have different Z laws, (1,0) and (1/2,1/2), but the possible result 0 is shared. Different laws alone do not give a certain answer from one record.

More strongly, two supplied pure states admit a measurement that always distinguishes them from one copy exactly when they are orthogonal. For necessity, group all outcomes decoded as the first state into an effect E, with 0≤E≤I. Certain discrimination requires ⟨ψ|E|ψ⟩=1 and ⟨φ|E|φ⟩=0. Positivity implies (I−E)|ψ⟩=0 and E|φ⟩=0: a positive operator with zero expectation annihilates the vector, by its positive square root. Hence ⟨ψ|φ⟩=⟨ψ|E|φ⟩=0. Conversely, projection onto ψ and its orthogonal complement distinguishes an orthogonal pair. This is the standard [nonorthogonal-state discrimination restriction](https://quantum.cloud.ibm.com/learning/en/courses/basics-of-quantum-information/quantum-circuits/limitations-on-quantum-information), with its operator proof supplied here. An inconclusive outcome or a nonzero error probability changes the task.

The restriction extends from identifying a state to identifying any exact classical answer q about it. If one fixed measurement and decoder always answer q on a supplied pure-state domain, nonorthogonal states must have equal q-values, by the same grouped-effect argument. Consequently q is constant on every component of that domain's nonorthogonality graph. On the full set of pure qubit states this graph is connected: nonorthogonal pairs are adjacent, while orthogonal ψ and φ both overlap (ψ+φ)/√2. Therefore every such universally certain answer from one unknown pure qubit is constant. Restricting the preparation domain to a known orthogonal pair permits a nonconstant answer. For any fixed finite number k of independently prepared copies of each candidate, a nonzero overlap remains nonzero after taking its kth power, so the full-domain exact discrimination obstruction persists even for collective measurements.

Seeing Is Encoding SiE9 expresses the corresponding fixed finite-outcome condition through overlapping response supports. That condition also applies to classical noisy measurements. The quantum model adds restrictions on which measurements exist, supplied here by positive effects and the Hilbert-space geometry. Neither the overlap condition nor a generic VI candidate set derives that geometry, interference or the Born rule. Classical probability embeds in the diagonal case: diagonal states ρ=diag(pᵢ) and effects E_z=diag(m(z|i)) give Tr(E_zρ)=Σᵢm(z|i)pᵢ. This recovers the classical response law without equating a quantum state with a hidden classical label.

IiS12 makes the scope of a uniform answer concrete. For every fixed reference phase, its two product preparations are orthogonal. Across unknown phases the answer families contain a nonorthogonal pair, excluding one universally certain reader. A positive candidate that passes every promised preparation gives a negative complementary weight on an input available through the same family's filtering. Directly preparing the relative distinction in orthogonal symmetry sectors admits a certain reading on the same carrier. The preparation relation changes which knowledge can be acquired. It does not turn an invariant label on descriptions into an automatically available operation.

The accessible system is part of each statement. If an orthogonal preparation record is retained elsewhere inside the same whole and becomes accessible, reading that record can identify the preparation even when the qubit alone cannot. Adjoining a fixed, uncorrelated apparatus state adds no such label and preserves the initial overlap. Thus the absence of an exterior does not make every internal distinction available to every reader. These are explicit mathematical model results. The current Swift quantum examples do not yet encode the general operator positivity argument or a calibrated quantum instrument. IiS12 now includes the source-bound finite negative-weight counterexample in its type checks.

### Crystallization

A discretized lattice can supply a finite configuration model if its site values are also finite. One can then ask which configurations a chosen observation distinguishes and whether a specified dynamics reaches a stable answer. An energy weighting alone does not construct an append-only record of eliminated configurations: a positive Boltzmann weight is not exclusion. The earlier ice table did not verify the retention, monotone elimination or unique-configuration premises. This remains a possible finite modeling exercise, not evidence that physical crystallization executes the VI acquisition loop.

**Local ice rule and global compatibility.** In the finite Vacuum/In/Out grammar, induction makes InCount and OutCount the counts of the two direction symbols. IceVertex now requires both counts to equal Two. Previously it required only InCount, admitting two-bond and five-bond expressions as vertices. Both were accepted by the old compiler check and are refused after the correction. Two of each symbol gives length four and exactly six ordered words: choose the two inward positions among four. This is the completeness argument for the six supplied aliases, rather than completeness inferred from a list's length.

For a finite loopless four-regular graph G with N vertices and 2N edges, give each edge one orientation. Its endpoint flags must be complementary: inward at one end is outward at the other. Let Ω(G) contain the orientations with indegree two at every vertex. Restriction maps Ω(G) into the product of the six local pattern sets. Its image is exactly the families agreeing on every shared edge. It is injective because a compatible endpoint flag fixes each edge, and surjective onto that compatible family because these choices reconstruct all edges and preserve every local rule. Thus local rules plus actual shared-edge compatibility characterize the global domain exactly. Local rule satisfaction alone does not supply the compatibility.

These global constraints alone already exclude uniqueness on a nonempty graph, without counting all configurations. Each connected component has an Euler circuit because every degree is even. Orient its edges along that circuit. Every visit pairs an arrival with a departure, so indegree and outdegree are both two at each vertex. This constructs an element of Ω(G). Reversing every edge constructs another element: it exchanges the two equal degrees and changes every oriented edge. Reversal is an involution with no fixed configuration, so |Ω(G)| is positive and even. This concerns configurations on the specified graph, before any quotient by graph symmetries. Further constraints can break the reversal symmetry. The proof supplies existence and nonuniqueness, without claiming an extensive entropy or a physical equilibrium result.

For example, after choosing a reference direction on each edge, write b∈{0,1}^{2N} for its orientation bits. Then the exact finite count is

```
|Ω(G)| = Σ_b Π_v 1[indegree_b(v)=2].
```

Under the uniform law on a nonempty Ω(G), the configuration entropy is k_B ln|Ω(G)|. A nonuniform law requires its weights instead (M32, M63). Replacing the simultaneous vertex conditions by independent probabilities gives 2^(2N)(6/16)^N=(3/2)^N, the Pauling estimate. The independence step is an approximation, as discussed in [Pauling's paper](https://elearning.uniroma1.it/pluginfile.php/656178/mod_resource/content/1/Pauling1935.pdf). [Lieb's exact square-ice result](https://harvest.aps.org/v2/journals/articles/10.1103/PhysRev.162.162/fulltext) has growth factor (4/3)^(3/2), with its two-dimensional lattice and thermodynamic limit. That factor is not an exact enumeration of ordinary hexagonal ice. The type-level local rule proves neither global count nor an equilibrium phase diagram. The retained graph and its edge relations are all internal to the model, without an external reservoir.

**M68 (which edge readings remove every alternative).** Fix a finite loopless graph G, allowing distinct parallel edges, and a prescribed outdegree α(v) at every vertex. Let Ωα(G) be its admitted orientations. Each edge is one addressed object shared by its endpoints. Reconnecting two edges can preserve all degrees while changing G, so degree-preserving rewiring is not an operation on this fixed domain.

Two members x,y of Ωα(G) differ on an edge set D. Orient D as in x. At any vertex, let a of these edges leave and b enter. Reversing D changes its full outdegree by b−a. Both full outdegrees equal α(v), so a=b. Every nonempty finite balanced directed graph contains a directed cycle: follow outgoing edges until a vertex repeats, using balance to rule out an endpoint with no outgoing edge. Remove the resulting cycle and repeat. This partitions D into edge-disjoint directed cycles. Reversing them one at a time changes x to y, preserving all degrees and the original edges at every step. This is the standard cycle-flip argument for prescribed-degree orientations, stated in [Aichholzer et al., §2](https://arxiv.org/html/1902.06103v1#S2), applied here to the identification question.

Now fix a set F of measured edges and retain their directions from an actual x. Its compatible alternatives are the members of Ωα(G) agreeing with x on F. The same proof connects any two such alternatives using cycles entirely outside F. Consequently,

```
x is the only compatible orientation
    iff the directed graph x restricted to E(G)\F has no directed cycle.
```

If a directed cycle remains, its reversal is a distinct compatible orientation. If another orientation remains, its nonempty difference from x contains such a cycle. Thus an instance certificate must meet every directed cycle of the actual x. For a narrower question q, retained readings determine q exactly when q is unchanged by every admitted directed-cycle reversal in this compatible family. Closure under all these reversals is what makes this local invariance test complete. Restricting the operation to short cycles or adding other constraints requires a new connectivity argument.

For ice, the uniform question has a stronger graph-only answer. More generally, suppose every degree of G is even and α(v)=degree(v)/2. Then

```
reading F identifies every member of Ωα(G)
    iff the undirected graph (V(G), E(G)\F) is a forest.
```

A forest contains no possible directed cycle, which proves sufficiency. Conversely, suppose an unmeasured undirected cycle C remains. A pair of parallel edges may form a cycle of length two. Orient C cyclically. Removing its edges leaves every degree even, so Euler circuits orient each remaining nontrivial component with equal in- and outdegrees. Together these orientations give x∈Ωα(G). Reversing C gives a different y with exactly the same readings on F. This proves necessity without assuming independent vertex patterns. It also shows why the even-degree balanced hypothesis matters: arbitrary prescribed degrees need not permit either cyclic orientation of a given undirected cycle.

The inclusion-minimal uniformly sufficient F are therefore exactly the complements of spanning forests of G. Indeed, deleting any member of F adds that edge to its complementary forest. Minimality says each such addition creates a cycle, which is precisely maximality of the forest inside each connected component. With n vertices, m edges and c connected components, all these minimal reading sets have m−n+c members. In four-regular ice this is n+c. There are generally several such sets, giving a concrete instance of VC84's alternative sufficient supports. The bound concerns fixed selections of individual edge readings, without an adaptive policy or a different measurement interface.

This also gives a constructive decoder on its admitted inputs. Choose the complementary spanning forest. At a leaf, all incident directions except its one remaining forest edge are known. Its prescribed degree forces that direction. Pass that direction to the other endpoint of the same edge, delete the leaf edge and continue. The finite forest guarantees termination. Existence follows here from the premise that the readings came from an admitted orientation. Arbitrary assigned pin bits can instead demand an impossible degree and must be rejected. Actual shared-edge incidence, leaf removal and that admissibility condition are the relations a static implementation must preserve.

For this spanning choice, the final root of each component supplies no independent balance constraint. Sum outdegree minus indegree over that whole component: each retained edge contributes +1 at its tail and −1 at its head, so the sum is zero. If every nonroot vertex is balanced, its root is balanced too. Thus n−c vertex balances force the last c, provided every edge really has its two stated incidences. A duplicated or missing endpoint invalidates the cancellation. If the unmeasured forest instead has several trees within one component, its remaining roots can have compensating nonzero balances and still need checking. This is conservation inside the specified graph, without an exterior to absorb a missing incidence.

Instance and uniform requirements differ. On two vertices joined by four addressed parallel edges, a balanced orientation has two edges in each direction. Measuring the two that point from the first vertex to the second forces the remaining pair. Its residual directed graph is acyclic, although its residual undirected graph has a two-edge cycle. The same two selected addresses fail uniformly: when their measured directions oppose, the remaining pair has two possible orientations. Any three addresses suffice uniformly, in agreement with m−n+c=3. This is a worked boundary of the general proof, not its basis.

The injection into m−n+c recorded bits gives |Ωα(G)|≤2^(m−n+c). For four-regular G and any probability law on its admitted configurations, Shannon configuration entropy obeys H≤log₂|Ω(G)|≤n+c bits. The bit bound is not an exact count: not every pin assignment need extend to an orientation. It supplies neither a probability law nor a thermodynamic identification. For a fixed cycle C, reverse it when it is directed and leave the configuration unchanged otherwise. This update is an involution on Ωα(G), so it preserves the Shannon entropy of a transported distribution on complete configurations. Conditioning on an observed event is a different operation. The local ice constraint alone supplies no preferred direction of time or entropy increase.

`IceIncidence.swift` implements the addressed leaf step. A port is `(vertex, slot)`, and a directed edge is `(tail port, head port)`. `EmptyIcePorts` starts at slot zero. `InAtIcePort` and `OutAtIcePort` require a reading from the same source and context whose actual head or tail matches the next port. Each constructor advances that slot and extends the existing In/Out word. A repeated slot or another vertex cannot substitute for the required incidence. The finite constructor grammar supplies this interpretation. An arbitrary `KnownIcePorts` or `PredicateReading` conformance does not establish it by name.

`IceLeafIn` and `IceLeafOut` read the remaining reference edge from that same source, require its local end at slot three, and derive its directed endpoint pair from the counts of the three known incidences. Completing the word derives a `Survivor`, without a supplied final direction. The inferred reading can enter the opposite constructor at the remote vertex, where its remote slot must also match. The original reference and source remain retained. Eliminating the unknown direction does not require deleting the edge from the whole. The output answers the declared completion question. Relating it to an unmeasured physical direction requires that source to satisfy the declared graph constraints. This layout places a nonroot vertex's parent edge at slot three, so a certificate for another forest must bind its actual port layout.

`Checks/IceIncidence.swift` checks both generic branches, transfer to the actual neighboring port, and two successive inferences inside one larger source using `SequentialReading`. It also closes one complete four-regular component: K5 with four leaves around a center. Six measured leaf-to-leaf edges each appear once in the source and are read at both endpoints. The four remaining star edges are inferred. Their same inferred readings supply the center's four ports and derive its two-in/two-out rule without another measurement. The catalogue below reconstructs the declared source from those same readings, replacing a separate hand-written reconstruction check. All five vertices reach four inspected ports. The incidence assignment therefore uses every one of this graph's twenty ports. This establishes the given completion, not the set of all orientations of K5 or a Swift proof for every graph.

**Static incidence coverage.** `CandidateRemoval` extends the existing SelectCandidateLeaf/Left/Right paths with their remaining support and address. The leaf case returns Never as the remainder. Each branch case preserves the fork and the untouched sibling while using its child's remainder. Induction on the finite path proves that exactly the selected occurrence is removed. The address records left/right choices and ignores payloads, so removing a sibling does not rename the surviving occurrence. `EmptyCandidateSupport` recognizes no remaining leaf in the finite Never/CandidateFork grammar, including padded empty forks. It does not require all such empty expressions to equal Never.

`AllocateIceVertex` uses this removal path to allocate the four different slots of its selected vertex address. Its state retains the original vertex declaration beside the remaining vertices and allocated ports. Start with the whole declaration and an empty port inventory, compose valid steps through `SequentialReading`, and require no vertex to remain. Every original leaf position is then used once: a removed leaf cannot be selected from the subsequent remainder. Different positions have different path addresses even when their payloads are identical. Each contributes four different slot addresses, so the final port inventory has no duplicate port. `AllocatedIcePorts` checks the initial and final boundaries of this construction. IdentityReading supplies the empty sequence when the declaration has no vertices.

These addresses belong to the retained declaration. They are not absolute names for physical objects, and equal path words in unrelated declarations do not establish physical identity. The certificate retains the declaration and binds the allocated ports to the actual incidence source and context being checked.

`ConsumeIceEdge` removes one actual edge application from a declared catalogue, then removes its actual tail and head from the port inventory in that order. The second port selection must start from the first remainder. Its output retains the same reading source and supplies both reduced inventories to the next step. `ExhaustedIceIncidence` binds the initial ports to the completed allocation, matches their contexts, and requires both final inventories to contain no leaf. Within the finite allocation, removal, identity and sequential-composition grammar, this proves that each catalogued edge occurs once and every allocated port occurs at exactly one edge end. No edge end can be spent twice, no vertex can be silently omitted, and equal vertex payloads cannot merge their addresses. With n allocated vertices, the catalogue therefore has 2n edges. This certifies a closed four-regular directed multigraph at its ports. Balance and absence of loops are additional properties of the declared graph.
